  5. **The PVRINOX defect is NOT gone, and the OCR path alone cannot remove it** (stop-and-describe rule; no
     `reality_engine/` change made). In the rebuilt store `fin_quarter_current` has no `revenue_from_operations` for
     Q2FY27 in either basis and consolidated `exceptional_items` is still 3,511 mn (p9); `pilot3_fin` returns
     `found=false`. Three independent causes: (a) `comparatives` reads `page.get_text("words")` from the PDF, never
     `doc_page` or `ocr_page` -- 12 results pages changed in `pages.db` and `fin_quarter_printed` is row-for-row the
     same (2,184/1,971/1,248 rows, all three stores; PVRINOX Q2FY27: 18 printed rows, no revenue), so
     `--ocr-db`/`page_atom` cannot help; (b) the `exceptional_items` row is a **wrapped printed label**: p9 prints
     "Profit before share in net profit /(loss) of joint venture," then "3 exceptional items and tax (1-2) 3,511 ...",
     and the continuation line matches `exceptional_items` alone -- this reproduces in the PDF layer AND the OCR words,
     so the garbled layer was never its cause; (c) the TextRecognizer truncates p3's label to "Revenue fro" (the page
     prints "Revenue from operations", 20,620 read correctly; total income 20,907 = 20,620 + other income 287) and
     reads p9's current-quarter revenue as "1,051" (the printed value is 21,051: 6-month 37,273 = 21,051 + 16,222,
     and total income 21,393 = 21,051 + other income 342), so feeding OCR words as-is would store a 20x-wrong
     consolidated revenue. `v2/page_words.words_for` also calls p3/p9 `ok` (its share heuristics), so the T-189 route
     returns the garbled PDF words for them. Needed: (1) `comparatives._extract_symbol` to take its words from
     `v2.page_words.words_for(store, doc_key, page, ocr_dbs)` and use the OCR words when the dictionary score is under
     the threshold, with `build.py` passing its `ocr_inputs` to `comparatives.run`; (2) join a wrapped label line to
     the row it belongs to before `match_line` (a general rule, any company); (3) make `page_words.quality` use the
     dictionary score, not only its share heuristics; (4) the durable Q2FY27 fix is the NSE XBRL filing --
     `results_xbrl.db` holds 566 filings for 2026-09-30 but only 2026-03-31 and 2026-06-30 for INE191H01014, so a fetch
     is missing, not a parse. `.tmp/prove_fix.py` prints both word sources through `comparatives.read_page` as the
     evidence for (b) and (c). Round 3 implements (1)-(3); the XBRL fetch (4) is still missing but no longer needed
     for the printed revenue.
- **T-208 round 3: the printed reader takes OCR words for garbled pages and joins wrapped labels (14 Nov 2026).** **Live rebuild (Wolf, 14 Nov 05:00):** after the merge each s10 store was backed up as `<n>_before_r3_2026-11-14.db`, then `build --part comparatives|preferred|ratios --ocr-db <n>/ocr/ocr_textrec.db`, then `stores|preferred|ratios` again to take the Q2FY27 XBRL that T-212 fetched (all rc=0). PVRINOX Q2FY27 `fin_quarter_current`: revenue 20,620 mn SA / 21,051 mn consolidated, PAT 2,532 / 2,631; `fin_quarter_xbrl` Q2FY27 22 SA + 23 consolidated rows in each of the three stores. `bluestar.db` rebuilt 06:25 the same way (backup `bluestar_before_r3_2026-11-14.db`, `--ocr-db ocr/ocr_bluestar.db`, comparatives/stores/preferred/ratios rc=0): Q2FY27 consolidated `exceptional_items` now 23.02 cr (was 133.61, the PBT line), revenue 2,930.95 cr unchanged.
  Code: `reality_engine/v2/page_words.py` (`dictionary`, `dictionary_score`, `quality`), `reality_engine/pilot3/comparatives.py`
  (`match_line`, `_fuzzy_score`, `_prefix_score`, `_continues`, `_data_labels`, `read_page`, `_table_rows`, `run`,
  `_extract_symbol`), `reality_engine/pilot3/build.py` (passes `store_db=out` and `ocr_inputs(out, ocr_dbs)`),
  test `reality_engine/tests/test_comparatives_wrapped_ocr.py` (6 tests).
  1. **Dictionary-score rule (`page_words.quality`).** A page token is CLEAN when it is a clean number, a word of the
     build's label lexicon (`pilot3/lexicon/*.yaml` + `business_abbrev.txt`) or a word of `pilot3/en_vocab.txt`; score
     = clean / scored tokens; single characters and bare separators are not scored; fewer than 30 scored tokens is
     `too_short` and not judged. **Threshold 0.75** (same as round 2). Measured on word boxes: PVRINOX `fa10203c` p3
     0.47 and p9 0.60 (both `jumbled`; round 2's page-text score with the docs/tasks prose corpus gave 0.59/0.78 --
     `en_vocab.txt` stands in for that corpus so a build needs no checkout). Below the threshold `words_for` returns
     the `ocr_page` words.
  2. **`comparatives` reads those words.** `run(..., store_db=<db>, ocr_dbs=<ocr dbs>)`; for each results page
     `_extract_symbol` asks `page_words.words_for`; when the text layer scores under the threshold and OCR exists, the
     text layer's own table leads and the OCR table only FILLS items it lost (an OCR cell never replaces a printed
     cell); a page with no text layer is read from OCR alone. Every OCR-read cell is gated: it is stored only when the
     page's own arithmetic or the XBRL confirms it (`ocr_unreconciled` in the review CSV otherwise), and a gated
     `revenue_from_operations` is dropped before the existing total-income-minus-other-income derivation runs. No
     coverage-row bookkeeping is touched, so a right printed row cannot be lost to an OCR guess.
  3. **Wrapped labels.** `_data_labels` joins a label line that prints no number to the next numbered row when the
     next label starts mid-phrase (a lower-case first word or `and`/`or`/`of` after a line ending mid-sentence);
     `match_line` then matches the joined label with `anchor=2`, so a phrase that lives only in the continuation
     ("... exceptional items and tax") cannot name the row. All printed labels are matched with `anchor=2`: a phrase
     buried mid-label is not the row (`Profit/(Loss) after exceptional item and tax from discontinued operations` is
     not `exceptional_items`). A two-token pattern may not skip an alphabetic word longer than three characters
     (`Other comprehensive income` is not `other income`), while OCR split noise (`tol 11 expenses`) still matches;
     an OCR-truncated label may match the START of a pattern (`Revenue fro` -> `revenue from operations`) and its
     figure is kept only when the arithmetic confirms it.
  4. **Tests.** `python -m pytest reality_engine/tests/test_comparatives_wrapped_ocr.py -q` -> 6 passed (wrapped
     label joins; p9's profit line is not `exceptional_items`; a page under the dictionary threshold takes its OCR
     words; an OCR figure that breaks the arithmetic is not stored, the derived one is; a truncated label is kept only
     when its figure reconciles). Existing `test_pilot3_comparatives.py` 21 passed, `test_v2_page_words.py` 5 passed.
  5. **Proof on copies** (`python -m reality_engine.scripts.store_diff`, `--parts comparatives,preferred,ratios`,
     `--base <main checkout> --head .`; the sandbox cannot create `git worktree` under the main repo's `.git`, so the
     main checkout path is passed instead of `main`; the store's `ocr_textrec.db`/`ocr_bluestar.db` is copied beside
     each copy as `<stem>_ocr.db` because the diff's rebuild passes no `--ocr-db`):
     - PVRINOX (`--target pvrinox`, `.tmp/store_diff_t208r3_12`): `fin_quarter_printed` 1,248 -> 1,270 (15 removed,
       37 added, 1.20 %), `ratio_panel` 541 -> 551 (1 changed), gates 0, **no longer served 0**. `fin_quarter_current`
       Q2FY27: standalone `revenue_from_operations` **20,620 mn (p3)**, consolidated **21,051 mn (p9)**, both
       `sum_ok`; `exceptional_items` **3,511 gone** (and 4,267). The consolidated figure is derived from the page's
       own arithmetic (21,393 - 342); the OCR's `1,051` was rejected by `total_income = revenue + other_income` and by
       `current + preceding = ytd`, and the standalone 20,620 is the printed row behind the truncated label.
     - KEI (`.tmp/store_diff_t208r3_13`): 2,184 -> 2,172 (21 removed, 9 added, 0.96 %), gates 0, no longer served 0.
       Removed: 3 mixed-unit `total_income` note rows and 5 tiny `tax`/`other_income` note rows that were unchecked
       duplicates; 5 `tax` rows CHANGED to the correct XBRL-confirmed values (e.g. Q1FY27 current 5.59 -> 950.24 mn)
       and the `11 Other Comprehensive Income/(Loss)` row no longer lands as `other_income`.
     - MEDANTA (`.tmp/store_diff_t208r3_14`): 1,971 -> 2,082 (30 removed, 141 added, 1.52 %), gates 0, no longer
       served 0; 108 new keys, all OCR-filled `sum_ok` rows from a page that printed (e.g. page 15: pbt/pat/tax/
       total comprehensive income for H1FY23 and FY23). The "removed" rows are whole-row hash changes on the same key
       (added twin rows), not lost keys; 3 unchecked `exceptional_items` rows taken from a "Profit before ... exceptional
       items and tax" line are gone (same defect as 3,511).
     - BLUESTAR (`.tmp/store_diff_t208r3_15`): 1,850 -> 1,839 (28 removed, 17 added, 1.51 %), gates 0, no longer
       served 0. `exceptional_items` now comes from the real "Exceptional items (refer note 4)" row (Q2FY27 current
       133.61 -> 23.02, H1FY27 259.23 -> 32.19, Q1FY27 preceding 125.62 -> 9.17) instead of the "Profit before share
       of profit of joint ventures, exceptional items and tax" line; two `pbt_before_exceptional` rows are added from
       that line; mixed-unit note rows (page 7/12) are gone.
     The four stores' only remaining unchecked losses are mixed-unit note rows and the removed `exceptional_items`
     lines above; every store reports 0 rows the serving tools would no longer return. The `ratios` part changes only
     PVRINOX's `pat_before_exceptional_ttm` (2.99 -> 6.50 bn, the bogus 3,511 no longer subtracted).
  6. **Open.** The NSE XBRL filing for PVRINOX 2026-09-30 is still missing (`results_xbrl.db`), so the current
     revenue is `fin_quarter_printed`, not XBRL. Untested on the remaining stores: the dictionary threshold may flag
     other jumbled pages and the OCR fill will then add rows (all gated); run the same store_diff before merging.

## 5. Code tools: outline and typecheck (T-054, 4 Nov 2026)

Retired 4 Nov 2026 (moved to the Recycle Bin, history in `tasks/archive/code-intel_install/`): the `plugins/code-intel` plugin — its AST index and CLI, and the stdio server's core, LSP client and server modules — and its test module.

`plugins/code-tools/` replaces the retired code-intel LSP. A resident `basedpyright-langserver` cost ~800 MB per worker and code-intel answered 1.5% of tool calls (423 of 28,787 over 207 runs); the two tools here run and exit, so nothing stays resident between calls.

- `outline(file)` — `plugins/code-tools/tools.py` walks one file with Python `ast` and returns every class, function and method with `name`, `kind`, `line`, `end_line`, the parent class and the first docstring line.
- `typecheck(files)` — one `basedpyright --outputjson` run with the checkout's `pyrightconfig.json` (120 s cap, the tree killed on timeout), returning `{errors: [{file, line, message}], error_count, seconds}`.

The MCP server is `plugins/code-tools/server.py` (`mcp__code-tools__outline`, `mcp__code-tools__typecheck`; `CODE_TOOLS_ROOT` sets the checkout); `plugins/code-tools/ct.py` is the CLI form the briefs name as the fallback. Wiring: `reality_engine/scripts/run_deepseek_harness.py` (`code_tools_patch` re-roots it at a worktree worker's checkout), `reality_engine/scripts/hp_worker.py` (`mcp_patch`), `.mcp.json`, `pyrightconfig.json`, `reality_engine/scripts/build_worker_context.py` (the pack's section 1) and `plugins/dsh-worker/server/core.py` (`TOOL_INSTRUCTION`); `plugins/dsh-worker/server/progress.py` counts `mcp__code-tools__*` as read-only. `plugins/dsh-worker/server/context_pack.py` now requires the `Evidence:` brief field (seven fields, `Evidence:` after `Accept:`), and `plugins/dsh-worker/server/plugin_server.py` states the seven in the `start_worker` description. The dsh profiles take the row in `tasks/dsh_profiles/code-tools.insert.yml`, replacing the old `mcp-code-intel` block. Tests: `plugins/code-tools/tests/test_code_tools.py`.

## 6. Loop checks: the night loop's frozen evals (T-055, 4 Nov 2026)

`reality_engine/night_eval/` (standard library + sqlite3, no model or network call) turns `tasks/night_loop/GOAL.md` into commands. `qset.py check <dir>` runs the line schema and Phase 1's Q1 sector fit (every `sector_fit` metric and row-need metric in that sector's list of `tasks/night_loop/questions/sector_vocab.json`, i.e. its own ids plus `universal` and `technical`), Q2 coverage (per company: each lens >= 10, orders 1st >= 40 %, 2nd >= 25 %, 3rd >= 10 %, needed document types) and Q3 (order-1 questions need >= 3 variations; two questions of the same symbol above 0.8 normalised token Jaccard are near-duplicates). `qset.py split <dir>` is the fixed split `int(sha256(qid)[:8],16) % 10 < 7` = dev, writing `dev.jsonl` beside the set and hidden to `.tmp/night_eval/hidden.jsonl` (the wrapper moves it; a hidden question is never printed). `answerable.py` scores A1 per need -- a typed-table row (symbol, period, basis, metric, `known_at <= as_of`; a need marked `new_table` names a Phase 2 table and is met only once the db holds it with a row for the symbol) or the `pilot3_search` bm25 page search called in-process (`plugins/reality-data/server/lookups_pilot3.py`, never through the MCP server) -- and lists the dev unmet needs by `table.metric` / `doc:<type>` as the next loop's work list; `--hidden` prints counts only. `guards.py` measures A3 provenance (value in quote, quote's first 80 normalised characters on the cited `doc_page`, `page_missing` counted separately) on the rows a branch added, A4 counts (XBRL disagreement of deck/release/transcript revenue-PAT-EBITDA at the total dimension, kpi periods starting after the document date, `known_at` before the filing, duplicate `doc.sha256`, rows per page above p99, margins > 100 %), A5 (the changed `build.py` parts on a COPY under `.tmp` with a `sitecustomize` that makes every socket connect raise, plus added lines under `reality_engine/pilot3/` scanned for company literals, ISINs, BSE codes, raw-data paths and hashes, plus the protected paths), A6 (the wrapper's test result, `--a6 ok`) and A7 (pages read and build seconds from `--timing`). `decide.py` prints ACCEPT only when A1-dev rises, A1-hidden and every company hold, A3 >= 98 %, A4 counts do not rise, A5 passes and A6 is green, with A7 allowed to rise <= 20 % only with an A1-dev gain of a point. `run_loop.py` launches one hidden `claude -p` session through `reality_engine/scripts/session_runner.py` (Opus, effort medium, <= 150 turns, 2 h, `--disallowedTools` Agent, the worker merge/push tools and `git push`/`git merge`) and prints exit, turns, tokens, `limited`; `audit.py` scans transcripts for reads of `hidden.jsonl` or `D:\RealityEngine\pilot3\answer_run*`. Commands and thresholds: `reality_engine/night_eval/README.md`. Tests: `reality_engine/tests/test_night_eval.py` (fixture sqlite databases, six-question sector sets and a temporary git repository under `.tmp`; `tmp_path` is denied in the dsh sandbox).

Known baseline on the 4 Nov live db (sample, `guards --limit 40`): A3 67 % with 40 cited pages absent from `doc_page` -- the annual-report rows (`fin_annual_line`, `doc_key` like `49222`) cite AR pages the pages part has not stored, so A3 on those rows cannot pass until the ARs are paged. A4 on the same db: 0 duplicate `doc.sha256`, 0 margins > 100 %, 0 periods starting after the document date, 2065 XBRL disagreements (the store carries several conflicting KPI rows per company/period) -- all comparable numbers for "not up".

### O11. pilot3 segments from the raw results XBRL (loop 2, 4 Nov 2026)
Script: `reality_engine/pilot3/xbrl_segments.py`; run it with `python -m reality_engine.pilot3.build --profile <json> --part segments --out <db>`
(`run(db_path, symbols)` returns files, rows, single-segment rows and check failures per symbol). The XBRL files are read through `raw_store` (one `prefetch` per company): since T-102 they live on Drive, and a run that skipped missing local files silently wrote 0 segment rows (8 Nov rebuild lost 1,334 kpi rows until this fix). Why: the results
store keeps only undimensioned totals, so per-segment values were lost at parse time. How:
1. File list from `results_xbrl.db.filings` by the company's ISINs and `period_end >= window_start`; a revision of the
   same period and basis keeps the latest `dissem_ts|broadcast_ts`. Raw files from `raw_market/nse_results_xbrl`.
2. Each context is read for axis, member and period (ids vary by filing). Concepts: `SegmentRevenue`,
   `SegmentProfitLossBeforeTaxAndFinanceCosts`, `SegmentAssets`, `SegmentLiabilities`; life insurers sum net premium,
   `IncomeFormInvestments`, `OtherIncome`, `TransferOfFundsFromShareholdersAccount` (`is_reported` 0) and use `SurplusDeficit`
   as EBIT. Only contexts ending on the filing's period end count. Quarter, H1/H2, 9M and FY contexts all become rows.
3. `Single segment` filings (flag read from the file) write `dimension='single_segment'`, not a filler.
4. Names: leading `(a)` dropped, punctuation collapsed, mapped per company (`_canonical`): names that occur together in one filing never merge; similar names (ratio >= 0.85) that never occur together merge to the spelling in the most filings (tie: latest filing); `raw_label` keeps the name as filed. A single-segment description of 'NA' or empty gives the label "Single segment".
5. `kpi.run` deletes only rows whose `source_type` is not `xbrl_segment`, so a later kpi rebuild keeps them.
6. Check 3 (`reconcile`): segment revenue less `InterSegmentRevenue` against `SegmentRevenueFromOperations`, 1%.
Result on the pilot10 copy: 10 companies, 0 check-3 failures, A1 571 -> 601. Tests: `reality_engine/tests/test_pilot3_xbrl_segments.py`.
Known limits: `kpi_series.value` for these rows is rupees as filed (`unit` inr, scale 1), unlike deck rows in crore.

### O12. Calculator tool and the answer number check (T-072, 5 Nov 2026)
31 of the 120 answer misses in the 600 hand-read answers were a worker's head sum or a ten-times unit slip while the data
was right (a market cap of Rs 32.20 lakh crore for 3.22; gross NPAs of Rs 3,574 crore for 35,736). Two pieces:
1. `pilot3_calc` (reality-data MCP, `plugins/reality-data/server/calc.py`): `+ - * / **`, parentheses and
   `sum/avg/min/max/round` over named inputs with units, walked from the AST (never `eval`). Inputs normalise once to a
   base unit (rupees, count, or a fraction for percent) and compute exactly with `Fraction`, so a unit mistake is an error
   message and never a number: subtracting shares from rupees or multiplying two rupee amounts fails. A money or count
   answer also prints rupees, crore, lakh crore and Indian digit grouping; a percent prints bps. One percentage minus another (T-109, 8 Nov) returns unit `percentage_points` with
   `percentage_points`, `bps` and `relative_change_pct` (27.09% -> 27.44% is +0.35 pp, +1.29% relative), so an answer never
   writes a pp move as a percent. `indian_grouping` is the
   12,34,567 form the answers need. Tests: `plugins/reality-data/tests/test_calc.py` (13).
2. `reality_engine/pilot3/number_check.py`:
   `python -m reality_engine.pilot3.number_check <answers.jsonl | answer.json | dir> [--tsv out.tsv] [--dev dev.tsv]`.
   Reason codes, each its own: `formula_mismatch` (a number's `formula`+`inputs`, or an older `computed: ...` source, does
   not recompute beyond its printed rounding; a source is read WHOLE or not at all -- `^` is an exponent, a root is
   evaluated, a fragment or an unparseable source is a warning, and a fraction is compared at both scales so
   `6,975/6,016-1` = 15.9% still fails 18.4% while `33,986/29,474` passes 115), `unit_slip` (a text amount matching a
   `numbers` entry only at 10x/100x/1000x, or a stated sum/ratio/percent that misses by such a factor; a two-significant-
   digit amount fires only when the answer prints that entry exactly nearby -- two printed digits are chance),
   `stated_working` ("A covers B 101.6 times", "X% of Y is Z", "from A to B, a fall of C", "N% below Y", "A + B = C";
   dates and years are never operands, a sum is read at both scales, a chain with `less`/`minus` is read whole or
   skipped, and a from-to percentage allows the endpoints' own printed rounding), `quote_mismatch` (only when the same
   digits carry a different value behind a different scale word; a value printed literally passes, a computed or ratio
   figure is not judged, and a value merely absent from a cut quote is a WARNING), `source_scale_mismatch` (T-129: a
   money entry's own `source` prints the entry's digits behind a different explicit scale word and the wrong amount --
   MARUTI-127's 4,136 crore sourced as "Rs 4,136 million"; the real amount, 413.6 crore, passes), `bad_grouping` (comma grouping
   neither Indian nor Western). `unmatched_amount` is a WARNING, not a block: prose quotes rounded intermediates an
   answer never lists, and blocking on those fails most honest answers. `--tsv` writes `file, qid, code, severity,
   detail, where`, one row per finding.
   `reality_engine/pilot3/answer_record.py` takes the same CLI as `record.py` (`<SYM> start <qid>`, `<SYM> answer <file>`,
   writing `.tmp/<SYM>_answers.jsonl`) and refuses a failing answer: failures printed, exit 1, a `check_failed` row
   appended; a retry carrying `"check_override": "<reason>"` records with `check_failures` and `check_override` attached.
3. Replay on 5 Nov (round 2) over the ten files of `D:\RealityEngine\pilot10\answer_a2`: 600 answers, 26 flagged, 37
   failing findings (unit_slip 24, formula_mismatch 10, stated_working 3); that run contains the 10 known misses. On the
   256-row dev set
   (`t072_dev.tsv`): 10 of 16 `arith_miss` caught (ETERNAL-057, ETERNAL-124 via sums, HDFCBANK-022/030/033/097,
   SUNPHARMA-116, INFY-019, KEC-119, BAJFINANCE-123, SBILIFE-117) and 3 of 240 graded-right answers flagged: two are real
   slips in the numbers list the grader missed (a gross-premium row ten times short of its own source; components ten times
   the answer text) and one is a false alarm (43.6 lakh crore of earning assets beside a 4,360-crore per-10bps row whose
   unit text quotes the same digits). Missed, and why: ETERNAL-082 (a market cap with no `numbers` entry and no stated
   formula), ETERNAL-124 (the sum has an unstated base), ETERNAL-126 (a 20%-of-volume scaling over a base in another
   sentence), HDFCBANK-063 (the percentage sits 57 characters before "from" and the same sentence holds a second range),
   KEC-109 (an H2 per-quarter division), KEC-110 (the bps claim's operand pair cannot be bound: 7-7.5% against 5.0% gives
   the 200-250 bps the text states). Tests: `reality_engine/tests/test_pilot3_number_check.py` (16).

### O13. Second reading an answer: its prose against its own numbers (T-083, 5 Nov 2026)
The re-run hand read put 89 of the 136 misses on a misread of the answer's own material. Three of those classes a
careful reader sees without the store: prose that contradicts its own figures (a growth series called "halving" when it
fell about a third; a margin series called "steady" while it falls), a cause with no figure, quote or named source, and an
answer that does not fit its question (four quarters asked and two given, a change dated before the window asked, a
standalone figure set against a consolidated one). `number_check.py` owns the arithmetic and is unchanged; this step
reads prose only and fixes nothing.
1. `reality_engine/pilot3/second_read.py`:
   `python -m reality_engine.pilot3.second_read --answers-dir <dir> --qids <file> --out <flags.jsonl>
   [--pass-no N] [--parallel N] [--limit N]`. It reads the `<SYM>_answers.jsonl` `event=answer` row for each qid (the
   last one when a qid has two) and `<SYM>_questions.json`, and sends the question, the prose and the answer's own
   `numbers` rows (value, unit, period, source, quote, `formula`/`inputs`; 40 rows, quotes cut at 200 characters) to
   `beam-free` (`reasoning_effort` low, 4000 output tokens, `x-opencode-session` from `go_session.session_id` on one
   stable job name per pass and the pool lane, `record_usage` to `tasks/ledgers/spend.jsonl`). Resumable: a `qid` +
   `pass_no` already in `--out` is skipped, so pass 2 is the same command with `--pass-no 2`. One row per answer:
   `qid, pass_no, status, flags, dropped_flags, in_tok, out_tok, finish, attempts, error`.
2. Flags carry `kind` = `contradicts_own_numbers` | `unsourced_cause` | `question_misfit`, plus `claim` and `why`. A
   script check, not the model: `claim` must be a contiguous span of the answer prose after whitespace normalising
   (case-insensitive, wrapping quotation marks ignored); a flag that fails is dropped and counted in `dropped_flags`,
   never kept. A `question_misfit` about something missing may leave `claim` empty only when its `why` names a content
   word of the question. A truncated or unparseable reply is retried once and then recorded as `truncated` /
   `unparseable` / `api_error` / `rate_limited` -- never as "no flags"; HTTP 402 or a billing body stops the run
   (exit 3), and a missing key exits 2 before any call.
3. Dev run on `D:\RealityEngine\pilot10\answer_a3\t083_dev.tsv` (46 `read_miss`, 233 `right`, 25 `arith_miss` /
   `absent_miss` / `store_miss` that this step cannot see): pass 1 flagged 28 of the 46 `read_miss` answers, 19 of them
   for the graded fault (41%, target 50%), and 43 of the 233 `right` answers. Read flag by flag, those 43 hold 22 false
   alarms (9.4% of `right`, inside the 10% target) and 21 real slips the grade missed: a count ("six consecutive
   quarters"/"four targets") larger than the answer's own list, a quote attributed to the wrong call date, an operating
   margin labelled an EBITDA margin, a consolidated ratio set against a standalone one. Pass 2 agreed with pass 1 on the
   flagged / not-flagged verdict for 261 of 304 answers (85.9%); pass 1 cost 606,448 input and 207,505 output tokens
   (free model, ledger still records the tokens at $0). A single pass is a sample: the model is not deterministic.
   Three prompt rounds were run; round 3 is the best and is the prompt in the module. Kinds it cannot see: an item the
   store holds and the answer says it does not (a deck page, a macro series), a data fact the answer alone cannot show
   (a terminology error, an index move, a 200-day average before the price history starts), a cost the store exposes
   through period/basis labels the `numbers` rows did not keep, and a count of periods split across two answers.
   Tests: `reality_engine/tests/test_pilot3_second_read.py` (17, fake client, no network).
4. Hold-out, read by Opus (`answer_a3\t083_holdout_qids.txt`, 296 answers; flags in `answer_a3\second_read\`, the
   worker's dev files in its `worker_dev` folder): pass 1 flagged 22 of 43 `read` misses, 16 for the graded fault
   against a pass mark of 18, so the test failed; nothing calls this module in the answer path. The numbers in item 3
   are the worker's own reads. Same-sentence flags given for another reason count in the 16 (three of them). Pass 2
   adds one more answer. The 39 flagged right answers split into 18 false alarms, 11 clear slips and 10 unsupported
   causes; 21 of the 39 were read from the flag alone.

### O14. Financial-line answers: one preferred figure per period (T-088, 6 Nov 2026)
Store fixes 1 and 2 of the T-087 report. Six general rules, no company name in code; the measured results are in
`docs/PILOT3_BLUEPRINT.md` section 9d.
1. **Preferred comparative, `lookups_pilot3.fin`** (`_preferred`, `_printed_cell_ok`). A quarter's rows are grouped by
   (measure, period, basis) and ONE row returns: the newest filing known at `as_of`, `preferred: true`,
   `restated: true` when it differs from the first printing, and `alternatives` = the other distinct values with
   `filing_date`, `role` (current / preceding / year_ago / xbrl), `definition` and `check_status`. Tie on the filing
   date: XBRL > `xbrl_ok` > `repeat_ok` > `sum_ok` > a clean unchecked cell. A printed cell that failed its check can
   still be a genuine restatement (Bajaj Q2 FY26 19,992.38), so it is admitted when its `raw_token` is one printed
   number; a token carrying letters or a currency word is refused (`displayable_printed`, T-090). The `as_of` cut happens before the election, so a date before
   the restatement gets the older value. Annual rows pass through unchanged (their `definition` already separates
   reported / derived / company-stated).
2. **Same quarter for growth, `ratios._printed_year_ago`.** The base is the current filing's own `year_ago` cell
   for that line when its check is `xbrl_ok`, `repeat_ok` or `sum_ok` (`sum_ok` means the year-ago column added up,
   which is the comparative a growth uses: HINDUNILVR Q2 FY26 15,919 in the Q2 FY27 filing; its first printing
   16,241 is pre-restatement, T-088 round 2). Where one filing printed two cells for the line the better check wins
   (`xbrl_ok` > `repeat_ok` > `sum_ok`), so INFY's cash-flow "Total income" `sum_ok` cannot beat the XBRL-confirmed
   revenue cell. The cell's `fiscal_period` must equal the year-ago quarter. With no such cell the printed quarter
   store (`_quarters`) is used and the base ref is suffixed `@fallback` (38 rows on the copy); QoQ's preceding
   quarter is label-checked too, and a mismatch refuses the row rather than using another quarter (`_fiscal_q`).
   INFY Q2 FY27 consolidated 8.276 -> 10.6855%; HINDUNILVR Q2 FY27 14.4921 and Q1 FY27 10.0527 (restated bases).
3. **Stated and derived together, `lookups_pilot3.ratio`** (`_stated_ratios`). The `ratio_panel` rows carry
   `definition: derived` with formula and input refs; the company's own figure for the same ratio in `kpi_series`
   (the metric name or its `_pct` form) returns beside it as `definition: company_stated` with quote and page, for
   every period asked. Company rows only (`entity` = symbol), the consolidated basis first, then `conf_rank` and
   `known_at`; a subsidiary's figure is never the company's.
4. **Entity apart from basis, `kpi_series.entity`.** `kpi.py` adds the `entity` column (`SCHEMA`, `NEW_COLS`, `COLS`,
   `_row_entity`, `_row_basis`): the company's symbol or the subsidiary the row's dimension / page heading /
   paragraph names (`_entity_tokens` holds the lexicon's entity tokens and printed names, so a segment word like
   'commercial' is never an entity). `basis` only ever says consolidated / standalone / not_stated (the subsidiary
   token that used to live there moves to `entity`; a basis word stored in `dimension` becomes `basis`).
   `_entity_before` returning a reporting-basis token (the company's own name) is ignored. `page_basis` now also
   reads an entity heading printed in the page's footer ("Executive summary - Bajaj Housing Finance"). `kpi()`
   normalises an older build's `basis` token to `entity`, lists the company's rows first, labels `entity` and
   collapses exact duplicates (same metric, entity, dimension, period, value, unit).
5. **Trailing twelve months, `ratios._panel`.** `pat_ttm` (`F_PAT_TTM`) and `pat_before_exceptional_ttm`
   (`F_PAT_BE_TTM`) for every company with the last four quarters; the row's `input_refs` are the four `pat`
   quarters, and the before-exceptional row appends each quarter holding a non-zero `exceptional_items` **with its
   amount** (`fq:SYM:Q3FY26:consolidated:exceptional_items=-2652200000`). `ratio()` parses `input_refs` into
   `inputs` for these two metrics.
6. **The two INFY annual-line claims, `stores.build_annual`.** `_concatenated_value` drops an unverified
   (`value_check='not_found'`) fact whose value matches none of the two or more numbers its `value_raw` prints
   (917,626,822,725 from "9,176 (FY26); 8,227 (FY25)"). `_ar_page_bases` maps the ar_facts document to its pages.db
   page (sha256) and `_statement_basis` reads "the Company" / "the Group" wording, overriding a stored basis that
   says consolidated for both the standalone and the consolidated note (3,749 -> standalone, 9,176 -> consolidated).
8. **The election in the data, `reality_engine/pilot3/preferred.py` (T-090).** The preferred-comparative rule of
   item 1 has one home now: `order_key` / `elect` / `evidence_rank` / `displayable_printed` are the moves of
   `_preferred` / `_evidence_rank` / `_printed_cell_ok`, and `lookups_pilot3.fin` calls `elect`, so the tool and the
   stored columns cannot drift. `preferred_from` / `preferred_to` on `fin_quarter_xbrl` and `fin_quarter_printed`
   give the window, in whole YYYY-MM-DD days, in which a row is THE figure: preferred at day D when `preferred_from <= D` and
   (`preferred_to IS NULL` or `preferred_to > D`), one row per (symbol, line_item, fiscal_period, basis) at any D, and
   `fin_quarter_current` is the row preferred now. The new build part `preferred` writes it: it ALTERs the columns onto
   an older database, runs for `profile.symbols()` (`--symbols` limits it), and costs **0.1 s** for the ten pilot
   companies (7,330 windows, 5,408 keys). Re-run it after any part that rewrites those rows (`stores`,
   `comparatives`). The tie-break ends with `-rowid` (the first stored row) so moving the rule changed no answer:
   on the copy, 3,295 quarterly keys compared old vs new are **0 changed**, and `pilot3_fin` vs the columns is
   **0 differences**. Annual lines are not elected: 67 keys hold more than one row but one filing's values share
   its `known_at`, so the rule would pick by rowid (reported, not forced).
7. **Rebuild a copy, never the live file.** `stores` uses `schema.create`, which DROPS its tables: run
   `--part stores` for ALL companies (scope it with `--symbols` and the other companies' rows are deleted);
   `comparatives` and `kpi` rewrite one symbol at a time.
   ```
   # copy D:\RealityEngine\pilot10\pilot10.db -> .tmp\pilot10_w.db (sqlite3 backup, read-only source)
   python -m reality_engine.pilot3.build --profile tasks/night_loop/pilot10_profile.json --part stores --out .tmp\pilot10_w.db
   python -m reality_engine.pilot3.build --profile tasks/night_loop/pilot10_profile.json --part comparatives --symbols INFY,BAJFINANCE --out .tmp\pilot10_w.db
    python -m reality_engine.pilot3.build --profile tasks/night_loop/pilot10_profile.json --part preferred --out .tmp\pilot10_w.db
   python -m reality_engine.pilot3.build --profile tasks/night_loop/pilot10_profile.json --part ratios --out .tmp\pilot10_w.db
   python -m reality_engine.pilot3.build --profile tasks/night_loop/pilot10_profile.json --part kpi --symbols BAJFINANCE --out .tmp\pilot10_w.db
   set REALITY_PILOT3_DB=.tmp\pilot10_w.db      # the MCP lookups then read the copy
   python -m pytest reality_engine/tests/test_pilot3_preferred_data.py -q    # 6 passed
    python -m pytest reality_engine/tests/test_pilot3_preferred.py -q          # 11 passed
   python -m reality_engine.pilot3.checkpages run                             # 146 found (29/59/58), default pilot3.db
   ```
   `checkpages run --db .tmp/pilot10_w.db` finds 0/314 because the fixture's 81 `doc_key`s are the
   `pilot3.db` keys while pilot10 stores `SYMBOL/family/uuid.pdf` (the untouched live pilot10.db gives 0
   too). `preferred` must be re-run after any later part that rewrites the two quarter tables.
   The live `pilot10.db` still needs `stores` (all ten), `comparatives` (INFY, BAJFINANCE), `preferred`, `ratios` and `kpi`
   (BAJFINANCE) before a question sees the fix, plus an MCP restart. Measured on the copy: FIN preferred 19,992.38 cr
   with 20,180.76 as its alternative (as_of 2026-01-01: 20,180.76); INFY Q2 FY27 YoY 10.6855%; Bajaj ROE Q2 FY27
   stated 20.6 / derived 17.914, FY26 stated 18.1 / derived 16.958, Q1 FY27 stated 20.4; `kpi('BAJFINANCE','roe_pct',
   'FY26')` 12 values with the company's 9 first and `entity` on every row; the Q1 FY27 "cost of funds was 7.21%" row
   is `bhfl`; PAT TTM 22,066.48 cr on four inputs and 22,331.70 cr before exceptional with Q3 FY26 named; FY26
   `lease_liabilities` 9,176 consolidated / 3,749 standalone. Growth: **1 value changed** from live (INFY Q2 FY27
   consolidated 8.2762 -> 10.6855, base 44,490 `year_ago` `xbrl_ok` of the same filing); HINDUNILVR is back on its
   live values (Q2 FY27 14.4921 = 18,226 / 15,919 `year_ago` `sum_ok`; Q1 FY27 10.0527 = 17,341 / 15,757; Q3 FY26
   5.6891 and Q4 FY26 -0.5474 also same-filing `year_ago` cells), as are the MARUTI and SUNPHARMA rows the first
   round moved. 38 growth rows carry an `@fallback` base ref. Tests in this sandbox: `test_pilot3_preferred.py` 11,
   `test_pilot3_kpi.py` 96 + 6 tmp_path
   fixture errors (sandbox), `test_pilot3_ratios.py` 4 + 8 tmp_path errors run with `.tmp` as the writable root: 8
   passed, `test_pilot3_comparatives.py` 21, `test_pilot3_core.py` 27 + 2 tmp_path errors (driver: 2 passed),
   `test_pilot3_xbrl_segments.py` driver 5 passed, `test_pilot3_checkpages.py` 4.

### O23. Linked and missing call transcripts (T-157, 9 Nov 2026)

19 of the 70 not-right pilot20 answers failed only because the call text was not held: one-page transcript cover
letters (Adani Ports 13, IHCL 2, Havells 1) whose page text or link annotation names the real PDF, and calls with
no transcript document at all (Pidilite Q2 FY27). `reality_engine/pilot3/linked_transcripts.py` resolves both
(`--store <db> --raw-out <dir> [--symbols ...] [--apply]`, report-only without `--apply`), stores each PDF through
`raw_store` and inserts `doc` + `doc_page` rows with the FTS kept in step; `--apply` on a copy of
`D:\RealityEngine\p20\p20new.db` added 14 of the 16 linked stubs and the PIDILITIND and APOLLOTYRE missing calls
(16 documents, 300 pages, 0 skipped). The method, the per-company table and the tests are in
`docs/PILOT3_BLUEPRINT.md` section 9l. Follow-up 9 Nov: `BSEClient.fetch_corporate_announcements` now reads every
50-row page up to `ROWCNT` (it read page 1 only), and `bse_candidates` also tries *Analyst / Investor Meet - Outcome*
PDFs that read as a call (HAVELLS files transcripts that way); 7 tests in `test_linked_transcripts.py` + 3 in
`test_bse_announcements_paging.py`. 16 held call transcripts were typed `announcement` across p20/u8/q12 (T-159); on
10 Nov 2026 `docs_collect` retypes 15 of them from their pages (`docs_collect.reconcile_doc`, section O3), measured
read-only against the four stores.


## 7. Run ledger (Shivam, 11 Nov 2026)

Why: progress stalled twice without a record of where the time and the errors went. Every run of every model is now
recorded in one SQLite file, `D:/RealityEngine/ledger/ledger.db`, by `reality_engine/scripts/run_ledger.py` (test
`reality_engine/tests/test_run_ledger.py`).

- **What is recorded.** `run`: one row per dsh/Beam worker or grader job, Claude session, answer run and reader run:
  model, host, ticket, start, seconds, outcome, tool calls in total and by tool, and how many went through the
  reality-data MCP tools (`mcp_calls`), raw SQL (`sql_calls`), or data read outside the MCP tools (`workaround_calls`:
  sqlite/duckdb opened from a shell, a raw PDF read), tool errors, file writes, model steps, tokens, and the review
  row from `tasks/efficiency/worker_reviews.jsonl`. `tool_call`: every call with its category and argument (300
  characters). `answer`: per question: tools, raw SQL used, seconds, grade, `workaround_reason`, `gap_class`,
  `gap_note`. `reader_page`: per page of a transcription run. `gap`: each gap found, its evidence, fix and ticket.
  `note`: disclosures and decisions (a test set seen by a fix, a score fitted to its own questions). `ingest_offset`:
  the byte offset, size and mtime each transcript/events file was ingested to, plus the carried-forward pending state
  (T-242).
- **When.** The project Stop hook (`session_hooks.py stop`) runs `run_ledger ingest-hook [transcript]` (every worker job,
  then the current Claude transcript) as one hidden child with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and no shell at each stop. The first
  version used `DETACHED_PROCESS` + a shell, which opened a console window on the MSI at every stop (fixed 11 Nov) (log `D:/RealityEngine/ledger/ingest.log`). Answer and reader runs are ingested
  when they finish (`ingest-answers <dir> --run-id --set`, `ingest-reader <dir>`); gaps and disclosures are written as
  they are found (`gap`, `note`).
- **Answer workers.** `pilot3/answer_record.py` refuses an answer that used raw SQL without `workaround_reason` (what
  the typed tools could not return); `gap_class` / `gap_note` name the data gap behind it. These fields reach the
  ledger's `answer` table.
- **Report.** `python -m reality_engine.scripts.run_ledger report` writes
  `D:/RealityEngine/reports/<date>_run_ledger.xlsx` (runs, by kind and model, answers, workaround reasons, gaps, gaps
  by class, reader pages, notes).
- **Backfill on 11 Nov.** 521 worker jobs (455 workers, 66 graders), 76 Claude sessions, 7 reader runs, 4 answer runs
  (T-179). Worker tool calls 57,596: MCP 5,704, raw SQL 4,534, data read outside the MCP tools 2,659.
- **Control-char regex fix (T-241, 24 Nov 2026).** `ingest_dsh` in `reality_engine/scripts/run_ledger.py` had
  literal `0x08` backspace bytes in `r"\x08grad(e|er|ing)\x08|review"` after a shell heredoc ate a backslash from `\b`,
  causing grader runs whose goal lacked `"review"` to be logged as `kind='dsh_worker'`. Replaced the `0x08` bytes with
  `\b` word boundaries, added a `dsh_grader` classification test in `reality_engine/tests/test_run_ledger.py`, and
  added `reality_engine/tests/test_no_control_chars.py` to scan every `*.py` file under `reality_engine/` and
  `plugins/` (skipping `.git`, `worktrees`, `__pycache__`, and venvs) and fail with `file:line` on any control byte in
  `0x00-0x08`, `0x0B`, `0x0C`, or `0x0E-0x1F`.
- **Incremental ingest (T-242, 26 Nov 2026).** `ingest_claude` and `ingest_dsh` in `reality_engine/scripts/run_ledger.py`
  stopped re-reading whole files at every stop (a session transcript is tens of MB; the ingest-hook process used ~13% CPU
  on 25 Nov). The `ingest_offset` table
  holds the byte offset each transcript/events file was read to, so only the appended tail is read with a binary seek
  and only complete lines are taken -- a half-written last line waits for the next stop. The run's calls, tokens,
  thinking steps, first/last timestamps and pending tool-call ids are carried forward in `state` (JSON) and merged with
  the new lines, so the rows equal a full re-read, including a `tool_result` written after an earlier stop closing its
  call. A file that shrank, or whose mtime went backwards, is taken as replaced: it is read from 0 and the run's rows
  are rewritten under the same keys, so ingests stay idempotent. Ceiling: a producer that rewrites a file in place to
  something larger is indistinguishable from an append (the session transcript and the dsh events file are both
  append-only today); add a leading-bytes fingerprint if that ever changes. Tests in
  `reality_engine/tests/test_run_ledger.py`: `test_ingest_claude_reads_only_appended_lines`,
  `test_ingest_claude_incremental_matches_a_full_ingest`,
  `test_ingest_claude_resets_when_the_transcript_is_replaced`, `test_ingest_dsh_reads_appended_events` (8 pass).

  in order, seeds `cNN/ocr/ocr_<SYM>.db` with one empty `ocr_page` row per `routes.csv` `needs_ocr` page (no or
  jumbled text layer; `ocr.pending()` alone sees only the no-text pages: c01 38 of 103), then `hp_ocr plan`,

  change is output-identical (c05 copy: 42 tables row-for-row equal, ids and part-stamp hashes aside; 80-page
  route CSV byte-equal). (1) `gap_loop.prepare` `results_quarterly`: SQLite does not push `WHERE isin` into the
  aggregate view `quarterly`, so each read aggregated all 111k filings of results_xbrl.db (13-47 s); the spec now
  writes `{view:quarterly where f.isin IN (...)}`, which `_filtered_view` expands into the view's own SELECT with
  the filter before its GROUP BY (0.02 s, same rows and types). (2) `comparatives._fuzzy_score`: 3.3 M
  SequenceMatcher calls were ~95 % of the part; `_ratio` caches the ratio and `_ratio_cap` skips a pair whose

  reads page_entities.yaml once per process (1,269 loads, 8 s). (4) `raw_store.RawStore` loads its 290k-row Drive
  manifest on the first lookup that needs it, not in `__init__` (3-16 s per process whose files are on disk),
  under a lock with the flag set after the load (the read-only review caught a race: a second page_reader thread
  saw an empty index during the first load; a failed load is retried on the next lookup).
  (5) `v2.page_route.run` downloads a Drive-only PDF at most once per document (`_pages_prefetched` inside the new
  `RawStore.keep()`: fetched on the first page that needs it, reused for the rest, deleted at the document's

  `RawStore.shared_cache()`, a thread-safe cache (per-file lock, in-use refcount, LRU cap), because `keep()`

- **T-214 step 3: the 10-company measured script-only build (15 Nov 2026).**
  `reality_engine/scripts/tg_measure_build.py` runs one company at a time through the T-208 recipe above

  `build --profile <json> --out <root>/cNN/cNN.db --part <part>` for the 13 parts), each step a subprocess with
  `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and no shell. No OCR, embedding or model read is run. Per step it records wall seconds, return
  code and the peak RSS of the child tree (psutil, one sample a second); a tree over `--ram-gb` (default 4 GB) is
  killed and recorded `ram_stop`, one over `--max-part-minutes` (default 40) is killed and the run stops. Each
  company gets `<root>/cNN/` (`cNN_profile.json`, `pages.db`, `cNN.db`, `b_<step>.log` per step, `routes.csv`,

- **Raw files to Drive without a build (`raw_only_to_drive`, T-241, Shivam 24 Nov 13:35: "Don't worry about the store

  takes every ISIN of the history fetch manifest with no `pending` row left and moves its `status='ok'` files still on

- **Ops gate: Shivam's operating rules as checks (T-241, 24 Nov 2026).** `reality_engine/scripts/ops_gate.py` reads
  `ops_rules.json` (each rule carries its source; edit only on Shivam's word) and prints one `RULE <name>: ...` line per
  breach (exit 1) or `ops_gate: ok`: `heartbeat` (no live `job_watch wait` of THIS account - waiter files record
  `owner`, the account's config folder - 25-minute heartbeats), `hy4` (the `hy4_always.sh` loop is not running, or
  `read_gate.pending_checkpoints` reports an ungraded checkpoint, or a failed one with no `ckpt_NNNN.fix.md` (under 24 h old) naming the fix and its owner for any output in `out_globs` or any `--out`
  of a running `v2.observations`, or two running readers share one `--out`), `idle` (no running dsh job off-peak; no running agy job in DeepSeek peak, Mon-Fri 06:30-09:30 and
  11:30-15:30 IST), `reportees` (more than 4 code workers; off-peak agy allowed since Shivam's 25 Nov ruling - `data_job` runs and read-only dsh jobs without a worktree, such as graders and audits, do not count, as in the dsh start gate, nor an agy job whose brief says `permission: read-only`; Eagle, 26 Nov), `quota` (an account's weekly use more than
  10 points behind a straight-line pace to its target - Wolf 95% by 25 Nov 23:59, Eagle 85% by 26 Nov 23:59 - or a
  5-hour reading at 90%: hand work over), `ssd_only` (a `.db` or `.db-wal` under `D:/RealityEngine`, up to 3 folders deep, changed in the last
  hour outside the allowed and skipped folders; junctions such as `tg_build` -> C: are not followed). The Stop hook (`session_hooks.py stop`) blocks once
  on these lines after its job checks; `cached_check` reuses a result for 5 minutes (`.tmp/ops_gate_<account>.json`); a
  fresh check takes 1-7 s. Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_ops_gate.py`.
- **hy4 raw replies kept (`page_reply`, T-241, 24 Nov 2026).** Every page `observations.run` reads stores the reader's raw
  JSONL reply in `page_reply(doc_key, page, fingerprint, raw, read_at)` (`fingerprint` = `<doc_type>:<read_gate
  fingerprint>` of the prompt that wrote it), so a parse or row-guard fix is re-applied offline by re-parsing, not by
  paying for the page again. Outputs read before 24 Nov 14:00 have no replies (the first v2 checkpoint, fy27_v2
  ckpt_0001, failed on number rows: 15/29 right, Wolf).
- **hy4 read gate: maker-checker-verifier (T-241, Shivam 24 Nov 2026).** `observations.apply_prompt_choice` sets `--transcript-prompt` on both `__main__` and the imported module, since `python -m` makes two module objects and the gate reads the imported one. `reality_engine/v2/read_gate.py`, called by
  `observations.run` (scoped `--only` runs too). (1) A run that would take an output DB's `page_read` ledger past 200
  pages needs, for each doc type, an entry in
  `reality_engine/v2/read_gates.json` at the CURRENT reader fingerprint (sha256 of `observations.template_for(doc_type)` + reader id):
  a `pass` from a reviewer who is not the prompt's author with >= 60 hand-read rows (`read_gate record`), or a
  `override` by Shivam with a reason, his quoted words (`--quote`) and optional `--scope` (the exact output DB stem, e.g. `obs_transcripts_fy27`; `_v2` is a different output) (a substring of the output DB path); a later `fail`
  withdraws a pass, and any prompt edit changes the fingerprint so earlier passes stop counting. (2) Every 2,000
  pages in an output DB's `page_read` ledger write `<out>.audit/ckpt_NNNN.jsonl` (60 seeded rows) and STOP the running
  read (`stopped`); the next run on that DB refuses to start until each checkpoint has a `pass` grade (`read_gate grade --ckpt ... --verdict ...`).
  A refused run prints `GATE: ...` and exits 3; `.tmp/hy4_always.sh` then moves to the next job. (3)
  `read_gate status --out <db>` shows gates, pending checkpoints and ledger counts; the managers' 25-minute heartbeats
  grade pending checkpoints (never the reader's author). First entries 24 Nov: transcript `fail` (Eagle, 110 rows:
  40 right) and Shivam's override scoped to `obs_transcripts_fy27`. Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_read_gate.py`.
- **hy4 resume and page ledger (T-241, 24 Nov 2026).** `--resume` now drops the pages already done *before* the
  `--limit` sample is drawn; it used to sample first, so a fixed `--seed` re-picked the same pages every run and the
  rest of the queue was never read (Eagle's audit: 56.2% of 36,767 transcript text pages had a row; 64 of 67 missed
  facts sat on pages with no row). Table `page_read(doc_key, page, outcome, rows, read_at)` records every page
  read: `rows`, `empty` (every row dropped: cover, disclaimer) or `skip` (nothing to read); those are not read
  again, while errors and parse failures stay unrecorded and are retried. Coverage = rows in `page_read`
  over the doc pages. Tests `test_ledger_pages_are_not_reread_but_errors_are`, `test_resume_samples_from_unread_pages_only`.
  T-242 (24 Nov, `reality_engine/v2/observations.py`): a transcript v3 window is ONE call over 5 pages, so a page
  with no row of its own (cover, disclaimer, registered office, signature) is `no_rows`, not a reply failure --
  `process_window` returns `reply_ok` (the CALL's reply parsed), `win_out_tok` and `last_row_page` per page, and
  `missing_pages` treats `no_rows` as done. Only a whole reply that is empty or unparsable, or a cut-off tail
  (`win_out_tok` >= 15,900 and the page at or after the last page that gave rows: ACCORD p10, a 65,606-char reply),
  counts as a parse failure and is retried. `reply_fail_share` = (parse_failures + errors) / pages ATTEMPTED
  (`read + no_rows + parse_failures + errors`), not / selected: the fy27_v3 run of 23:07-23:21 printed 0.145
  (392 of 2,701), and 390 of those 392 were pages inside a reply that parsed, 353 of them transcript pages 1-2.
  Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_transcript_window.py` (8).
- **Bulk hy4 reads fetch each Drive PDF once (T-242, 26 Nov 2026).** Four 30-worker readers measured 5-10
  pages/min while the model call was 7-13 s and rclone is fast: every page reached `raw_store.local` twice
  (`page_words.words_for` and `page_reader._page_image` -> `reference_pages`), so each page downloaded the whole
  deck/results PDF again, and 60 `rclone copy` processes were alive at once. `observations.run` now wraps its
  `ThreadPoolExecutor` in `raw_store.default_store().shared_cache()` and submits pages in `(doc_key, page)` order,
  so one document's pages are read while its one fetched copy is hot; the `--recheck` path keeps its
  `_kept_pages` -> `keep()` per document. `RawStore.shared_cache` is thread-safe where `keep()`/`prefetch()` are
  not (they swap instance state): a per-file lock, so concurrent callers of one file wait for the single fetch;
  an in-use refcount, so LRU eviction above `max_bytes` (3 GiB) never deletes a file a page is reading; and the
  `raw_fetch_*` folder deleted on exit, also on an exception, as the Drive-only rule requires. Outside the block
  `local` is unchanged. Tests `test_shared_cache_fetches_one_drive_pdf_once_for_eight_threads` (8 threads x 5
  pages = exactly 1 fetch), `test_shared_cache_evicts_the_least_recently_used_file_above_the_cap`,
  `test_shared_cache_removes_its_folder_on_exit_and_on_error`; `test_observations.py` 172 passed. Not measured on
  a live run yet.
- **hy4 output DB locking (T-241, 24 Nov 2026).** `v2/observations.py` opens its `--out` database with a 120 s
  sqlite timeout: a row count run against the live output DB once killed a run at commit ("database is locked").
  Read a live output DB with short queries only; `--resume` re-reads any page whose rows were not committed.
- **Transcript prompt v2 (T-241, 24 Nov 2026, Eagle).** `--transcript-prompt v2` (default `v1`) reads transcript
  pages with `PROMPT_TRANSCRIPT_V2`: the prompt is told the call's own quarter (`call_quarter(known_at)`: the last
  quarter that ended before the filing month, e.g. filed 2024-08-09 -> `Q1 FY25`) and resolves "the quarter",
  "this year", "H2", "next year" into fiscal labels (`Q1 FY25`, `H1 FY25`, `9M FY25`, `FY25`); a comparison figure
  keeps its own period; the printed words go to `period_source`. A number row now also carries `high_text`
  (-> `value_high`), `sign` (`-` -> negative `value`, `direction='down'`, only when the measure or unit is a movement:
  `SIGNED_MEASURE` = growth / change / yoy / qoq / bps, so a level such as inventory days is never negated) and
  `speaker`. The prompt drops analyst figures and arithmetic, dates, years, list markers, postcodes, ratings and
  hypotheticals as values, and sets basis only when the sentence says it. A v1 reply carries none of the new keys,
  so v1 rows are unchanged. A/B on 40 pages of 8 seed-241 calls (pages 3-7), 40 seeded number/guidance rows per arm
  hand-graded: v1 11 right / 21 partial / 5 wrong / 3 junk, v2 23 / 9 / 5 / 3; fiscal-label periods 21% -> 81% of
  rows, speaker on 0 -> 54%; fact recall on those pages 70 vs 69 of 72. After the first round the prompt also gives a comparison figure its own period (`Rs. 857 crores in H1 FY '24` -> `H1 FY24`) and drops dates as values; patched re-run (`ab_v2c`): 79% fiscal-label periods, speaker on 58%, 40 ranges with `value_high`, recall 69 of 72. Wolf's independent grade of v2c (60 rows, seed 2411): 32 right / 21 partial / 7 wrong / 0 junk, not a pass; all 7 wrongs were figures that are not the company's own. v2d adds `claim_type` (company_actual / company_target / third_party / hypothetical / expectation / ask): hypothetical, expectation and ask numbers are not stored (`NOT_A_COMPANY_FIGURE`; the statement keeps them), a third-party number gets `entity_scope='industry'`, and only a company_target is guidance. Also: a printed `%` keeps unit `%`; a transcript number takes the LAST standalone/consolidated word before it on the page (`section_basis`), then the reply's basis; `_scale_factor` reads 'lakh crore' as 1e12 (all readers; it returned no scale before because two words matched). Wolf's grade of v2d (60 fresh rows, seed 2412): 38 right / 13 partial / 4 wrong / 5 junk, fail against the proposed bar (wrong+junk <= 5%, right >= 70%). v2e: the prompt splits 'decreased by 8 to 69' into a level and a change and gives a 'from 12% to 20.4%' start value its earlier period; it bans counts (agendas, fronts), technical specs (160 km/h, 2x25kV) and vague durations; growth is a growth measure, a company-wide amount its level, revenue.mix / mix.share only for one named segment, product, geography or customer; a forward target is guidance; the speaker is whoever said the sentence. Script guards: a label ending in a change verb + 'by' ('have decreased by') turns a level measure into `NEW:<measure>_change` (negative on a fall; `BY_CHANGE_RE`); `section_basis` runs for every v2 transcript row (`transcript_v2`) and skips a comparison mention ('better than standalone performance', `COMPARED_BASIS_RE`). Three regexes (`\b` in `SIGNED_MEASURE`, `COMPARED_BASIS_RE`, `BY_CHANGE_RE`) had been written through a shell that ate one backslash and held backspace characters; a test now fails on any control character in the reader source. v2e re-run (`ab_v2e2`, 40 pages): fiscal periods 84%, speaker 63%, basis on 121 rows, ZENTEC's consolidated 158 cr / 64.70 cr now consolidated, MUFTI 'decreased by 8' = `NEW:inventory_days_change` -8, no spec or agenda counts, recall 69 of 72. The read gate (`read_gate.py`) needs an
  independent pass at v2's fingerprint before a bulk v2 read. Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_transcript_prompt_v2.py`.
- **Statement fixes from the v3 spot check (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Eagle).** At 1,436 pages of the live fy27_v3 run, verified statements held 79 with no speaker, 43 ending in '?' (analysts' questions) and 19 stored twice. `obs_speaker.attach_statement_speakers` now also marks a passage that ENDS in '?' unverified (`question_end`; a manager's "Why? Because ..." inside an answer is kept) and drops a second identical (doc, page, speaker, text) statement; `process_window` (the live v3 path) calls the same pass, which the 5e3e33e0 merge wired only into `process_page` and `--reparse`. Measured by `--reparse` of a copy (C:/RealityEngine/t242/v3_spot1_fix.db): verified statements 4,145 -> 3,973, verified with no speaker 79 -> 0, ending in '?' 43 -> 0, duplicates 19 -> 0. Tests in `test_obs_speaker.py`. Then (fy27_v3 ckpt_0001 grade, W = AMTECH p9 where the page's own turn labels are shifted): a statement whose speaker matches the doc's analyst roster and not the management pool is unverified `speaker_is_analyst` (the analyst list drops anyone the pool also holds); on the spot copy it unverifies 6 statements, all analysts' (v3_spot1_fix2.db). And (394_v3 ckpt_0001 J, NLCINDIA p1): a statement on pages 1-2 worded as the filing letter ('for your information and record', 'kindly take on record', 'Regulation 30', 'enclosed herewith') is dropped (`_FILING_LINE_RE`); a company secretary's real opening remarks stay, since only the letter's wording counts (Wolf). On the live v3 DBs it matches 8 statements, all letter text. And (fy27_v3 ckpt_0002: 2 W were a speaker carried past an in-document hand-over, 1 J a greeting): every statement is checked against the printed label before it (`attribute_document`); when both the model's speaker and the label are full management names on the roster and differ, the label wins (`speaker_model` keeps the model's name). Without a roster nothing is relabelled: a hand-over just before the passage to someone else (`handover_conflict`: 'over to you, X', 'hand over to ... X', 'invite X'; nicknames and titles ignored) or a different last printed two-word label (`last_label_before`; analysts', moderators' and cover-letter labels ignored) makes the row unverified (`speaker_handover`, `speaker_label_conflict`). A passage made only of greetings, thanks and hand-overs is dropped (`greeting_only`); a sentence counts as a greeting only when what follows the phrase has no figure and at most 8 words ("Thank you, Vaibhav, it is approximately INR5,400 crores..." is kept), and call-logistics lines ("I'll come back into the queue", "that is all from my side", "all the best for the coming quarters") count too (394_v3 ckpt_0002 J; live v3 DBs: 36 of 39,512 statements). On the graded checkpoint: both W and the J fixed, 0 R lost; on the 1,436-page spot copy: 17 relabels, about 10 rows newly unverified, 4 greeting passages dropped (v3_spot1_fix5.db). And (fy27_v3 ckpt_0003, all 4 J boilerplate): `greeting_only` now also drops an opening welcome of any length when the sentence says 'welcome' and holds no figure (a fiscal-period label like Q2FY27/H1FY27 is not a figure), an optional leading Now/So/And/With that before a hand-over ('Now I hand it over to Mr. ...'), 'good night', thanks for joining/attending/taking the time, and closing housekeeping ('keep you posted', 'send your questions', 'audio recording/transcripts uploaded or available on the website', 'looking forward to', 'stay safe/healthy', 'I hope you ...'). Sentence splitting keeps a title's dot ('Mr. Sohoni') inside its sentence; only a short greeting's tail is length-checked, and any one sentence with a figure keeps the passage. Tests `test_ckpt_0003_housekeeping_and_welcome_passages_are_dropped` and `test_welcome_or_housekeeping_with_a_number_is_kept`; on the fy27_v3 DB it drops 10 of 15,517 verified statements (0.06%), all greeting/logistics, none an answer; transcript fingerprint stays 2fae7ddac53e (v3).
  - **Narrowed `question_end` (`obs_speaker.question_passage`, T-242, 25 Nov 2026).** A passage ending in `?` now unverifies only when it has <= 2 sentences or at least half its sentences end in `?` (a trailing courtesy/tag question like `right?`, `Is that helpful?`, `Am I able to answer your question?`, `Does that answer your question?`, `What was your (second/next) question?`, `isn't it?`, `no?` never counts towards the half), keeping multi-sentence management answers verified. Eagle review: a last sentence that checks a reading ("is that correct?", "is my understanding right?") always flags the passage (an analyst, Wolf 394 #80805), and "Have a good night" / "good night" is call logistics for `greeting_only`. On fy27_v3_r1.db 145 of the 298 question_end rows stay flagged.
  - **Pleasantries, question praise, defer/hand-over and generic speakers (`obs_speaker.py`, T-242, 25 Nov 2026).** 394 transcripts ckpt_0006 failed 48/8/1/3 on 3 J pleasantry/defer lines and 3 P generic/empty speakers stored verified. `_normalise_ligatures` folds PDF OCR ligatures (`ﬁ->fi`, `ﬂ->fl`, `ﬀ->ff`, `ﬃ->ffi`, `ﬄ->ffl`, `ﬅ/ﬆ->st`, `Ɵ/ɵ->ti`, `Ư->ff`) before matching without changing stored text. `greeting_only` now also drops figure-free sentences that are: thanks plus a welcome to participants or the call (`_THANKS_WELCOME_RE`), praise of a question (`_PRAISE_QUESTION_RE`), or a defer/hand-over (`_DEFER_HANDOVER_RE`: `defer to X`, `hand/pass over to X`, `request/ask/invite X to ...`, `let X answer/take`, `X will answer/take that`) with no substantive clause (`_SUBSTANTIVE_CLAUSE_RE`) after it. `resolve_speaker_name` (`_is_generic_speaker`) and `attach_statement_speakers` mark empty, `Management`, `Company`, `Moderator`, or bare-designation speakers (`CFO`, `ED (Finance)`, `CMD`, `Company Secretary`, etc.) `unverified` with `speaker_generic` unless resolved to a full management name. Measured on 300-page `--reparse` samples (stores untouched): `fy27_v3_r3.db` (305 pages, 1,482 statements) dropped 0, newly unverified 6 (`speaker_generic`); `tx394_ckpt6/snap.db` (318 pages, 1,714 statements) dropped 1 (0.058%), newly unverified 70 (`speaker_generic`); targeted `--reparse` of the 6 evidence calls (87 pages) dropped all 3 J lines and marked LLOYDSME/ASTRAMICRO `speaker_generic` while resolving KTKBANK #8996 to `Srikrishnan H`. Full-DB scan: 0 figure drops, 12/27,951 (0.043%) and 19/55,284 (0.034%) new drops, 0 R rows hit across `regrade_fy27_r3` (48 R), `tx394_ckpt6` (48 R) and `audit_tx394` (49 R); transcript v3 fingerprint unchanged (`2fae7ddac53e`). Tests in `test_obs_speaker.py`.
  - **Closing remarks and after-call deferrals (`obs_speaker.py` `greeting_only`, T-242, 25 Nov 2026, DeepSeek).** Each checkpoint still carried one J closing/deferral line because `greeting_only` needed EVERY sentence to be greeting/boilerplate, so a thanks or an after-call promise wrapped in confidence/governance platitudes survived. `greeting_only` now also drops a figure-free passage whose every sentence is a closing thanks (`_CLOSING_THANKS_RE`: "thank you all/one and all/everyone [for joining/for your time/for your questions]", "wish you (all) a good evening/day", "we look forward to interacting/speaking again next quarter"), a deferral (`_DEFERRAL_SENTENCE_RE`: "(we|I) will (get back|come back|revert) (to you)? (on that|offline|post this call|after the call)", "please reach out/get in touch with our IR/investor relations", "we can take it/this offline"; the preamble reuses `_DEFER_PREAMBLE`) or a generic closing platitude (`_CLOSING_PLATITUDE_RE`: confidence/positive/assurance/mindful/transparent/well-governed/team/committed/endeavour/... with no figure and no `_SUBSTANTIVE_CLAUSE_RE` match), with at least one closing/deferral sentence (`_closing_remarks_passage`). A figure or a substantive clause keeps the passage, and the deferral marker must be present, so "we will get back to growth of 15%" and "get back to growth" stay. Measured in-process over every statement (`tasks/t242_closing_measure.md`): `tx394_v3_r4.db` 55,219 statements -> 14 dropped (0.025%; 3 closing, 11 deferral); `fy27_v3_r3.db` 27,951 -> 14 dropped by the new rule (12 of those already dropped by the old rule, 2 incremental, 0.007%); 0 figure drops and 0 substantive-clause drops; 0 graded R dropped across `regrade_fy27_r3`, `regrade_tx394_r4`, `tx394_ckpt6`, `tx394_ckpt7`, `tx394_ckpt8` and `audit_tx394` (360 graded rows), while KTKBANK #56, ELLEN #17 and ANTHEM #10 are dropped; `audit_txfy27` has no grades file on this laptop, its #10 (the cited J) is dropped and its #17 (the P "But ..." outlook) is kept. The transcript page-prompt fingerprint `12e603811501` (`read_gate status`) and the v3 window prompt fingerprint `2fae7ddac53e` are unchanged (script only). Tests: `test_obs_speaker.py` (36 passed) and `test_v2_guards.py` (18 passed).
  - **Non-call documents, moderator speakers and short content-free remarks (T-242 surprise audit, 25 Nov 2026, DeepSeek).** A 60-row surprise audit of verified statements from the whole-universe transcript run (`C:/RealityEngine/t242/tx_fy27_surprise1/`, 45 R / 9 P / 2 W / 4 J, W+J 10%) found three shapes: a deck/AGM minutes read as a transcript, a company introduction stored under "Moderator", and short content-free remarks. `obs_speaker` now: (1) a transcript is a call only when `call_label_lines` counts at least 3 speaker-label lines across its pages (`Name:`, `Name - Designation:`, role labels `Analyst:`/`CS:`/`CMD:`, standalone names that recur or name a role, and auto-transcript labels with a timestamp before or after the name, `0:05 Ved Krishna:` / `[1:33] Pranay Pasricha:` / `Sachin Srivastava 0:10`); only the label part is checked for digits, never the speech after its colon. A document below 3 is `not_a_call` for every statement and `observations.transcript_pages` skips it. `_is_heading_label` rejects a slide/table heading ("Profit After Tax", "Revenue from Operations") as a speaker, `is_person_speaker` accepts a full name carrying its designation before the first comma/dash ("Mr. Sanjay Wadhwa - Chief Financial Officer, 360 ONE WAM Limited:") but rejects a date/place field word, and `extract_turns` no longer reads a dated line ("Sunday, September 27, 2026 at 11:30") as a designation turn. (2) a statement whose speaker is the moderator/operator (or a name on the document's moderator roster) is unverified `speaker_moderator`. (3) `short_remark_reason` drops a passage of at most 8 words with no figure, no substantive clause and no named entity when it is generic praise ("We have done well", "That's a good point"), a pointer to another speaker or question ("Kedar, I think his question was on products"), or a refer-back sentence ("So that's a meaningful swing", "That is the reason") whose content words are not in `question_context`. Measured in-process over every statement (`tasks/t242_surprise_measure.md`): `fy27_v3_r3.db` 27,951 statements -> 234 touched (0.837%; `not_a_call` 8 statements/3 docs, `speaker_moderator` 52/26, `speaker_not_a_person` 194/10, short remarks 147/81); `tx394_v3_r4.db` 55,219 -> 272 (0.493%; `not_a_call` 7/1, `speaker_moderator` 7/7, no heading speaker, short remarks 272/156). 0 graded R changed across `regrade_fy27_r3` (48 R), `tx394_ckpt6` (48), `audit_tx394` (49) and `tx_fy27_surprise1` (45); `regrade_tx394_r4`/`tx394_ckpt7`/`tx394_ckpt8` have no per-row grades file on this laptop and the rules change no row in them. All 6 evidence rows are dropped or unverified (scoped `--reparse` of the 6 documents, 108 pages). The doc check flags 29/389 fy27 and 25/907 tx394 documents (over the 3% report bar): all are cover letters, one-page exchange filings or empty-text scans, none a call; the 358 fy27 documents holding statements flag 3 (0.84%). v3 fp `2fae7ddac53e` unchanged. Tests in `test_obs_speaker.py` (AGM minutes, auto-transcript timestamps, role labels, heading speaker, moderator, short remarks, and the figure/product/referent counter-tests) and `test_observations.py` (the reader skips a non-call transcript).
- **Transcript prompt v3: windows of pages (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Eagle; Shivam's 17:15 test).** `--transcript-prompt v3 [--transcript-window 5]` reads a call in blocks of 5 pages (pages 1-5, 6-10, ...), one hy4 call per block, with the page before the block shown as CONTEXT ONLY and a header naming the company, call date, the quarter reported, the year-ago and previous quarter and the fiscal year (`PROMPT_TRANSCRIPT_V3` = `TRANSCRIPT_V3_HEAD` + the v2 rules with a `page` key on every object; fingerprint 2fae7ddac53e). `expand_transcript_windows` turns the sampled pages into whole blocks before the read gate counts them; `process_window` splits the reply by `page` into one result per page (its own JSONL in `page_reply.raw`, so `--reparse` rebuilds a page alone; v3 replies get the v2 guards), drops rows of the context page, rows with no page and duplicates, and books the call's tokens on the block's first page. Cost: about 1.2x the pages in input tokens (the context page), one call per 5 pages. Measured before it was built in (5 calls, a blind strict DeepSeek grade, 60 rows per method on the same 27 pages): statements 25/4/0/1 against page-by-page 21/7/2/0, numbers 20/3/7/0 against 18/3/9/0, speaker filled 100% against 76%; report D:/RealityEngine/reports/2026-11-24_whole_call_vs_page.html. A whole call in one call does not fit: a reply holds at most 16,000 output tokens and a 40-page call needs ~70,000. `process_window` passes the call's speaker roster (`doc_speakers`) so guard 14 sees it. Needs its own read-gate pass (60 statements from ~15 fresh calls, non-author) before a bulk run; tests `test_transcript_window.py`.
- **v2 transcript number guards and `--reparse` (T-241, 24 Nov 2026, Eagle).** Wolf's grade of the first fy27_v2 audit checkpoint (2,000 pages, v2e prompt) failed on numbers: 15 right of 29 (statements 30 of 31). Five script guards now run on every v2 transcript row (`transcript_v2`, or a reply carrying `claim_type`), code only, so the read-gate fingerprint stays `e95108f16e65`: (1) `negative_printed`: 'negative', 'loss of', 'minus', 'net loss' in the label or the 25 characters before the value negates a level row ('EBITDA stood at negative Rs27 crore' -> -27; never a measure named *loss* or a movement already signed); (2) `PERIOD_VALUE_RE`: a value that is a period label ('H1FY28', 'Q2', 'FY27') is dropped, numbers and guidance; (3) `is_cover_letter`: a page 1-2 with Scrip/Security Code, 'Sub:' and 'Dear Sir/Madam' gives no rows at all; (4) a `value_high` below the value is dropped ('20% or 10%'); (5) `printed_range_high`: with no `high_text`, the upper end printed right after the value ('$23 billion to $25 billion', '60% to 65%'; must exceed the low and be under 20x, and both ends share a unit: 'PAT rose by 11% to INR34.5 crores' is a growth and a level, never a range, a false range the first --reparse of 394_v2 caught) fills `value_high`, for guidance from its sentence first. `--reparse --baseline <db with page_reply> --out <copy> --stores ... [--only sel.json]` (`reparse_db`) rebuilds the selected transcript and annual-report pages' rows from the stored raw replies with today's parsing and guards, with no model call and no read gate; v2 guards apply when the reply's `page_reply.fingerprint` names the v2 prompt; `page_read` is updated; an annual-report page is rebuilt with the reader's own inputs (text layer, words, deck as-of/basis, the stored `page_ocr` text) and its statement lines then pass `apply_ar_statement_gate` (T-242, see the annual-report statement gate entry); deck pages are counted, not re-parsed (they also need words/OCR). Pages read before `page_reply` existed (fy27_v2 ckpt_0001) cannot be re-parsed. T-242 (Wolf's grade of the first re-parse, seed 2424: 40/15/2/3 of 60) added four more: (6) `inner_range`: a range printed inside one value_text ('40%-45%', '40-45%', 'INR310 to INR320') sets value and value_high (high must exceed the low, so '2024-25' is no range); (7) a number row whose label is only 'to', 'Rs.', 'INR', 'upto' (`BARE_LABEL`) AND whose value is printed right after a range start ('310 to INR320', `RANGE_START_RE`) repeats the range's upper end and is dropped; (8) `FRACTION_RE`: '1/3' is 33.33 %; (9) a `nim` row whose label or the 60 characters before it say cost of funds / borrowing at (`COST_OF_FUNDS_RE`) becomes `cost_of_funds`. Forward ranges stored as number rows ('will be 18-20%') need the prompt, not a script: left for v2f. Wolf's grade of the second re-parse (seed 31337: 38/15/3/4 of 60; verified rows alone 20/4/0/1) added: (10) `word_fraction`: '14 and a half percent' is 14.5 (also 'and a quarter', 'three quarters'); a range's sign flips as a whole, both from hy4's `sign` field and from guard 1, and guard 5 reads the printed range on the absolute low, so '25 to 30 bps reduction' is -30..-25, never -25..+30; (11) a `percent`-family measure whose value prints no %, bps, x or 'percent' within 14 characters after it gets the scale note `no_unit: a ratio printed without its %, bps or x` and is served unverified; (12) `FORWARD_CUE_RE` (`forward_number_cue`): a v2 transcript `number` row whose `printed_label` or preceding clause (up to 80 chars after the last `.`, `?`, `;` or ` and `, with no past-actual cue between the forward cue and the value) carries a forward cue converts to `record_type='guidance'` (unless `claim_type` is `third_party`/`hypothetical`, which stays `number`) and gets `forward_number: a stated future figure, served as guidance` so it stays `unverified`, while past-actual statements ("grew by", "we have done", "was ... last year") and cues in an earlier sentence stay plain `number` rows; (13) `industry_figure_cue`: a v2 transcript `number` row whose measure starts with `industry_` / `NEW:industry_` / `NEW:market_`, whose label or clause names a market/industry/macro metric (`INDUSTRY_CLAUSE_RE`, skipping `"our"` / `"market share"`), or that cites a named agency source near the value (`INDUSTRY_SOURCE_RE`) gets `entity_scope='industry'` and `industry_figure: a market/industry figure, not the company's` (`unverified`); (14) `not_management_speaker`: a v2 transcript `number` row whose `speaker` has a non-management role, is moderator/analyst-labelled, or was introduced by the moderator (`_ANALYST_INTRO_RE` / `QUESTION_WE_HAVE_INTRO_RE` on the page or `obs_speaker.build_doc_context` during `--reparse`) gets `not_management: said by a participant, not the company` (`unverified`); (15) `apply_measure_conflicts`: after all page rows are built, v2 `number` rows sharing `(value_text, value, sentence)` keep one row per measure and mark every row in multi-measure groups `unverified` with `measure_conflict: one printed figure mapped to several measures`; (16) `RANGE_TAIL_RE` / `printed_range_high` now allow a scale/percent unit word between the low value and `'to'` (`Rs.1200 Crores to 1300 Crores`, `18 percent to 20 percent`, `5 million to 6 million`) while enforcing unit agreement across both ends; (17) `transcript_bound_kind` (`UPPER_BOUND_TAIL_RE`, `LOWER_BOUND_TAIL_RE_V2`): a v2 transcript `number` row whose label or own preceding clause ends with a bound cue (`more than`, `over`, `at least`, `upwards of`, `in excess of`, `less than`, `below`, `up to`, `at most`, `not more than`, `maximum of`) sets `value_kind='lower_bound'` or `'upper_bound'` and marks it `unverified` in `evaluate_status`; (18) `BY_CHANGE_RE` / `ADDED_CHANGE_RE` + `QUARTER_CHANGE_AFTER_RE`: a v2 transcript level row printed after `increased/decreased by` (allowing trailing approximation/currency words) or `added|net additions of|incremental|addition of` followed by a quarterly period cue (`during/in the quarter`, `this quarter`, `in Q[1-4]`, `sequentially`, `QoQ`) converts to `NEW:<root>_change` with `direction='up'` for additions; (19) `transcript_adjusted_cue` (`TRANSCRIPT_ADJUSTED_RE`): a v2 transcript `number` row whose label or own clause carries an adjusted-basis cue (`excluding`/`exclude`, `ex-<item>` excluding delivery/corporate terms like `ex-factory`, `adjusted`, `pre-Ind AS 116`, `like-for-like`, `on a comparable basis`, `pro forma`) sets `adjusted=1` so `evaluate_status` marks it `unverified`. Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_v2_guards.py`.
