- **T-242: free vision-model OCR fill for the image-only deck pages (`scripts/ocr_fill.py`, 25-26 Nov 2026).**
  Selects every universe store's `routes.csv` row with `doc_type=deck`, `route=needs_ocr` and a reason starting
  `text layer none` (1,363 pages across 136 stores in `C:/RealityEngine/tg_build/universe_fy27`; `--symbols`,
  `--limit`, `--seed` narrow it), resolves the PDF through the store's `doc.source_key` and `raw_store` (Drive-only
  copies are prefetched for the run and deleted), renders a 150 dpi PNG (PyMuPDF), reads it with the bake-off prompt,
  and writes one `ocr_page` row per page whose `method` is the reading model's (`ocr_nemotron_vl` or, from 26 Nov,
  `ocr_deepseek_flash_vl`), `ocr_conf` NULL, `n_boxes` = line count and `words_json` one pseudo-word per line
  `[0, i*12, len(line), i*12+10, line]` (the model returns text, not boxes; `page_words.ocr_words` reads that shape,
  and `page_words.OCR_METHOD_PRIORITY` ranks both fills below `ocr_textrec`/`ocr_rapid`, nemotron ahead of deepseek).
  Rows go to `<out-dir>/<stem>_ocr.db` with `--out-dir` and beside the live store otherwise; `--resume` skips pages
  any ocr_fill model already read, and a page held under another method is never overwritten.
  Free-only: only the ids in `FREE_ROUTES` can be called (`kilo` over HTTP with `KILO_API_KEY` and
  `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-super-vl:free`; `zen` through `opencode run --standalone`, quality order
  `nemotron-3.5-super-vl-free` then `deepseek-v4.1-flash-free`), the paid `mimo`/`hy4-flash` ids are refused by name,
  and a non-zero `step_finish` cost stops the run; keys come from `resource_watchdog.user_env()` and are never
  printed. Model choice per call is the route's model with the lowest EWMA seconds/page (alpha 0.3) among the models
  not cooling down; unmeasured models are tried first in quality order and every 8th call on a route probes its
  least-recently-used model; a throttle cools that model alone (30 s, doubling to 600 s) and requeues the page.
  Concurrency is per route (AIMD): each route starts at `--start-workers` (6) calls in flight, gains one after 20
  successes in a row, loses 30% (floor one) on a throttle and is capped by `--max-workers` (24); threads = the sum of
  the caps, a thread holds a route slot for its call, and every change prints `limit <route> <old>-><new> (<reason>)`.
  The summary JSON adds `models` (per model: `ok`, `err`, `throttled`, `median_s`) and `limits` (final limit per
  route); the page line is `ok   <route>:<short model> <symbol> p<page> <s>s <chars> chars`, and `ocr_fill.jsonl`
  carries `route` and `model`. Two sandbox limits were measured 25 Nov: Zen's HTTP endpoint answers every free model
  with `FreeUsageLimitError` ("Free models work in OpenCode"), and the `opencode` CLI cannot open its log under
  `%USERPROFILE%\.local\share\opencode` inside the dsh sandbox (EPERM), so a sandboxed pilot runs `--routes kilo`;
  outside the sandbox both routes run. Python 3.14's `tempfile.mkdtemp` gives the folder a 0o700 DACL the restricted
  token cannot write into, so `_shared_acl_tempdirs()` creates `raw_store`'s prefetch folders with the parent's
  permissions for the run (a `raw_store` helper would be the general fix). Pilot (25 Nov, 40 pages, seed 1125,
  `--routes kilo`, `.tmp/ocr_pilot`): **40/40 written, 0 errors**, 32 stores, 578.1 s total, 14.5 s/page mean /
  10.7 s median, **6 pages with >= 4 figures**; per-page log `ocr_fill.jsonl`, 10 outputs `samples.txt`. Nothing was
  written beside a live store. Test: `python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_ocr_fill.py
  reality_engine/tests/test_v2_page_words.py` (**20 passed**: 15 + 5; the brief's `test_page_words.py` is
  `test_v2_page_words.py`). The bulk run waits for the 60-row grade.

## Modular store repair (T-218, 16 Nov 2026)

Shivam 16 Nov: repair one part of a built store; never rebuild the whole store. `reality_engine/pilot3/part_stamp.py`
writes one `part_stamp` row after every finished part (`part`, `code_hash`, `out_hash`, `inputs`, `built_at`,
`seconds`, `git_head`), and `build.py` stamps the part it ran (`extract` stamps `kpi` and `transcripts`). `code_hash`
covers `PART_FILES`: the part's module, the lexicon and map files it reads, and the helpers that decide its rows. A
shared file is hashed by the functions the part uses (`../gap_loop/prepare.py::store_copies,_read_sqlite`), so a
helper fix marks only the parts that call it; shared plumbing is left out, so a migration does not mark every store
stale. `out_hash` is an order-free hash of `PART_TABLES` (a `table-prefix*` drops the columns a later part writes);
`inputs` records the `out_hash` of each upstream part. `stale()` lists a part as `never stamped`, `code changed`,
`<part> output changed` or `after <part>`.

`reality_engine/scripts/store_repair.py` runs the named part and the parts that read from it, in build order:

```bash
python -m reality_engine.scripts.store_repair --stale --root D:/RealityEngine/tg_build
python -m reality_engine.scripts.store_repair --baseline auto --root <root> [--stores c01]
python -m reality_engine.scripts.store_repair --part guidance --root <root> --stores c01,c02 [--no-dependants] [--note R-003]
python -m reality_engine.scripts.store_repair --part stale --root <root> --stores c01 [--approve-loss]
python -m reality_engine.scripts.store_repair --accept <run id> --root <root> --stores c01
python -m reality_engine.scripts.store_repair note add|set|list ...
```

Shivam 17 Nov: every defect is scored and the fixes go in priority order. `note add` refuses a note without
`--kind wrong|corrupt|missed` and `--kind_why` (what the defect does to the data and why), `--companies_hit`,
`--companies_of` and `--hit_basis scan|trial|suspect` (how the companies were counted: a scan of every store's tables,
graded trial answers, or unconfirmed scan hits). `store_repair.scores` gives **severity** = companies hit of companies
counted, **criticality** = wrong 3 (a row or reply served as true is not: the answer is wrong without warning),
corrupt 2 (the source text is damaged, so rows read from it are lost or wrong), missed 1 (absent: the answer says not
found), and **priority** = criticality x (companies hit + trial questions failed). `note tally --graded
<trial>/graded.jsonl --map <trial>/note_map.json` (`{qid: note id}` for every non-right answer; the trial grader
names the note per answer, field `note`) writes `trial_failed` and `trial_companies` on each note and stops on a
failed answer with no note. `note list`, `INDEX.md` and the grid's notes sheet list live notes highest priority first.

- `--baseline <commit|auto>` stamps a store built before stamps existed with that commit's code. `auto` reads the
  store file's modified time, so give the commit when the file was touched since the build.
- `--part` needs `--stores`: one company at a time. A part runs when it is named, when it is stale at its turn, or when
  an earlier part that ran rewrites one of its tables (`part_stamp.rewrites`: `stores` -> `preferred`, `kpi` ->
  `segments`). `--no-dependants` runs the named parts only; `--part stale` re-runs what `--stale` lists.
- Gate: a failed part, or any table with a removed row or a lost served row, puts the store back and keeps the result
  as `repair/<run>/after.db` + `diff.json`, so `--accept <run id>` can put it in place after a hand-read.
  `--approve-loss` skips the row gate.
- `note add|set|list` keeps one entry per defect under `tasks/store_repair/` (`notes.jsonl` plus a generated
  `INDEX.md`); stores are named by build folder, never by company. Every repair and accept appends one line to
  `<root>/repair_log.jsonl`; database copies are kept for the 2 newest runs per store. `store_diff._checkpoint` waits
  up to 120 s and retries when another process holds a read lock, and if a store stays locked `store_repair` restores
  it, records `skipped: database locked` in `repair_log.jsonl`, and continues with the next store.
- Shivam 16 Nov: every fix is written up like one notebook cell. A note also carries `--code` (file::function,
  comma list: where the fix lives), `--tables` (the store tables it touches), `--tags` and `--scope general|company`
  (a rule for every company, or a fix that holds for one only). Shivam 16 Nov (evening): every patch is written up
  in five points, so a note also takes `--missing` (what the store or code lacked) and `--next` (what can follow).
  `INDEX.md` prints one card per note: 1 why it had to be done (the question), 2 what the problem was (symptom +
  root cause), 3 what was missing, 4 how it was fixed, 5 what can come next; then code, tables, tags, scope, stores
  repaired, runs. The grid's notes sheet and `items` (T-219) carry the two new fields. `note grid --root <root>` writes
  `reports/store_repair_grid.xlsx`: sheet `grid` has one row per store and one column per part (`ok` or
  `stale: <reason>`, plus the notes that repaired that cell), sheet `notes` every field of every note, and sheet
  `completeness` the sign-off counts per store (`store_repair.completeness`, read-only: XBRL and printed result
  quarters since 2024-04, shareholding quarters by source, transcripts, guidance rows, KPI verdicts, order wins,
  decks, annual reports, results documents, latest document and price dates; a missing table is blank, not 0).
  First read, 16 Nov: c09 has XBRL for 7 of 10 quarters; c10 has no XBRL and no shareholding (fetch gaps); c06 has
  11 transcripts and 0 guidance rows (R-001 worded path); c01 has no transcript (no call filed, R-002).
- First use on the ten measured stores in `D:/RealityEngine/tg_build/measure10`, baselined at the commits they were
  built from (c01-c07 `e48b848bd4`, c08-c10 `7616ee321c`): `--stale` listed exactly `profile` (fix C) and `stores`
  (fix A) with their readers. c02 ran `profile` + `stores` + `preferred`, skipped 12 parts and took 45.5 s against the
  263 s median full build, diff empty. The first c02 attempt was REFUSED: `stores` rewrote 450 `fin_quarter_xbrl` rows
  and `preferred` had been skipped, so its windows would have been lost; that is why `rewrites` exists. c01 with all
  15 parts forced: 187.7 s, diff empty (a rebuild is deterministic). c04: 135.2 s, 7 `kpi_series` rows added, 0
  removed. c03, c05, c06: 33-36 s, diff empty. c07: 258.3 s, 74 `shp_category` rows (fix A) and 65 `kpi_series` rows added, 0 removed. c08, c09, c10: 43-53 s, diff empty. All ten stores then read `up to date`.
- **Order wins (T-025, repair note R-003, 16 Nov).** `reality_engine/pilot3/order_wins.py` runs inside the `events` part and writes table `order_win` (schema version 11): one row per order a filing discloses, read from the Regulation 30 table SEBI prescribes by its printed labels (awarder, nature, terms, domestic/international, size, execution period, promoter interest, related party), so it holds for any issuer. Columns: `value_cr` or `value_usd_mn` with `value_text` and `value_source` (`table`, `body`, `title`), `duration_months` with `duration_text`, `seq` (a filing with two orders gives two rows), `title_value_cr` and `title_mismatch` (headline and body disagree), `quote`, `extractor_rule` (`order_table_v1`, `order_text_v1`). A court, tax, tribunal or regulator order is not a row; neither is a results press release; a field that runs into another label (scanned tables) stays empty; a cell with more than one duration gives no `duration_months`; a filing the feed repeats is stored once. Read with `pilot3_sql` (`SELECT announced, awarder, value_cr, duration_months FROM order_win`). Dry run on the ten measured stores (read-only): 19, 18, 2, 3, 1, 17, 10, 2, 1 and 17 rows; 10 rows carry no size. Written so far to store 1 only, through `store_repair --part stale` (2.5 s, 19 rows added, 0 removed); the other stores follow one at a time after a hand-read. Not yet covered: a contract 'executed' or 'extended' with no table and no winning verb in the headline. Tests: `python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_pilot3_order_wins.py` -> 7 passed.
- **Printed-results scale (repair note R-006, 16 Nov).** `comparatives._unit` now reads a page's unit only where the page declares it (`in lakhs`, `Rs. Crore`); a unit word after a figure is a note sentence. `_roles` tries the declared scale first and lets a scale the XBRL figures confirm beat it. Cause: on store 1, three results filings printed in lakhs had no unit line in the text layer and a note said `Rs. 20 crore`, so 342 `fin_quarter_printed` values on four pages were stored 100 times too large (full-year PAT 1,857.67 crore for 18.58). Those rows were the row checker's reference, so right KPI rows were quarantined as a part of the total. After the repair (`comparatives -> preferred -> ratios -> checks`, 44 s, refused by the gate because the table has no diff key, hand-read, accepted): all 342 values divided by exactly 100, 0 rows lost, 7 gained, `xbrl_ok` 451 -> 566, KPI verdicts quarantined 117 -> 85 and verified 13 -> 20. Unseen ten-company store (copy diff): 0 values changed, 0 rows lost. Stores 2-10: no value changed; on store 10 (no XBRL) the gate refused the first version, which dropped 89 rows of two scanned pages whose unit line reads `Rs on Lakhs` / `Rs. rnlakhs`, so a scan's misread `in` (up to three letters, never a digit) is accepted between the currency and the unit. Pilot stores are not re-run yet.
- **KPI page unit in lakhs (repair note R-005, 16 Nov).** `kpi.page_money_unit` read only billion/million declarations, so a bare table figure on a page printed `Rs. in Lakhs` was stored as crore (store 1: Q4FY24 'revenue 3,117.64 crore' for 31.18, net debt 29,132 crore for 291). It now reads lakh and crore declarations too (`_PAGE_UNITS`; only a declaration, never a unit word after a figure; two different declarations on a page = unsure, nothing changes), and `_page_unit_canon` applies `inr_lakh`. The row checker's `check_page_unit` compared units only, so it also quarantined a row converted correctly; it now passes a row whose stored value is exactly the printed number moved from the declared scale to the row's (`_rescaled_exactly`, 0.01% slack). Store 1 (`--part kpi,checks`, 25.6 s, refused, hand-read, accepted): 57 rows divided by exactly 100, 0 lost, 2 added; quarantined 85 -> 49, verified 20 -> 26, unverified 77 -> 109. The 49 still quarantined are segment revenue, standalone-for-consolidated and period-mapping conflicts the checker is right to stop. Tests: `test_pilot3_kpi.py` 114 passed, `test_row_checks.py` 72 passed.
- **Document map (Shivam 17 Nov).** `reality_engine/pilot3/doc_map.yaml` lists, per document type (annual report, results, presentation, call transcript), each section with its heading and row-label patterns, its usual position, the data items, the store table they must land in, the reader that fills it today (`NONE` where none) and a must-exist rule. `python -m reality_engine.pilot3.doc_map scan --root <stores>` labels every held page read-only into `<root>/../doc_map.db` (`page_section`, `section_rows`: rows the section's table holds for the annual report's fiscal year), and `report --out <xlsx>` writes sheets `map`, `gap_summary`, `coverage` and `gaps` (store x section x year printed but empty). First run on the 91 stores (`reports/2026-11-17_doc_map.xlsx`): the cash-flow statement is found in 92% of 270 annual reports, the PP&E note in 96%, the key-ratios note in 93%; printed but no rows: PP&E 239 store-years (85 stores), capital commitments 189 (72), CWIP 175 (64), revenue break-up 170 (60), finance costs 157 (61), segment note 126 (48), cash flow 80 (75), P&L 51, balance sheet 47. Balance sheet and P&L headings are found in only 60-64% of reports: the heading patterns need widening. Tests: `test_doc_map.py`.
- **KPI million spellings (repair note R-017, 17 Nov).** Trial 1 found 51 kpi rows in 10 stores printed in million but stored as crore ("Rs.20000 mns", "INR 1,900+ Mn", "EBITDA (C in Million) 1233.00"); the checker hid 45, but served 6 (order books 10 times too large) because `check_page_unit` skipped any row with a scale word near the number without comparing it. `kpi.VAL_RX` now reads `mns` and a `+` before the scale word, and `page_money_unit` reads "(C in ...)" (the rupee glyph read as a letter). `row_checks.check_page_unit` adds the hard rule `own_scale`: the scale printed at the number (`_own_scale_word`: right after it, or in the label just before it with no number between) must match the row unit unless the value was moved exactly (`_rescaled_exactly`, which now reads only the printed number, so "Rs. 193.76" is no longer read as ".193.76", and ignores a worded sign). Store 4 repaired (order book FY24 20,000 inr_mn = 2,000 crore; FY24 revenue recovered as verified). The re-run over the other 90 stores also applied the R-010 OCR cells to kpi for stores built before it, so 48 runs were refused by the row gate and wait for the R-013 hand-read. Tests: `test_pilot3_kpi.py` 115, `test_row_checks.py` 74 passed.
- **Share count is the parent's (repair note R-008, 16 Nov).** `stores._parent_share_count` drops a consolidated quarterly `share_capital` row (paid-up capital / face value) that is more than 1% from the standalone row of the same quarter end; a consolidated row with no standalone that quarter stays. Cause: SPMLINFRA's Q2FY27 consolidated XBRL filed paid-up capital Rs 13.88 lakh against Rs 17.83 crore standalone, so 694,000 shares were stored for 89.2 million. c07: 1 row removed, nothing else changed. Test: `test_pilot3_shp_drive.py`.
- **Transcript fetch family (repair note R-002, 16 Nov, approved by Shivam).** Of 37 queue companies whose store held no call transcript, the BSE feed (to 19 Oct) and the NSE listing (to 16 Nov) show transcripts for 3 (no BSE code, NSE only); 34 have filed none on either exchange since 2024-04, so their stores record no call filed rather than a gap. Steps: `nse_announcements --symbols <file> --out D:/RealityEngine/raw/nse_announcements/<run>` (listing only, 93 companies in 97 s), then `--attachments-only transcript`, which downloads from the stored JSONL only rows whose `record_kind` is `transcript` (600 files, 52 companies, 610 MB, 637 s; the wider `transcript` bucket held 1,673 rows, mostly meet notices and audio links); `docs_collect` already finds `nse_announcements/*/attachments/<SYM>`. `fetch_bse_announcements_daily --start 2026-10-19` refills the market-wide BSE feed, but it writes `analysis/bse_announcements.db`; the stores read `reality_engine/data/bse_announcements.db`, so the new rows are copied across (`INSERT OR IGNORE ... WHERE DissemDT >= <start>`; 16 Nov: 31,155 fetched in about 4 h, 35,336 rows copied, feed now to 2026-11-16 15:38). 34 queue companies filed a call transcript on BSE after 19 Oct (the Q2 FY27 calls); none of the 34 with no transcript did, which confirms them as no call filed. Raw files go to Drive with `raw_to_drive ... hash compare upload`; delete only after the stores are rebuilt. Test: `python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_nse_announcements.py` -> 29 passed.
- **Full shareholding pattern for any company (repair note R-004, 16 Nov).** `stores.build_shp` read only the local pilot folder `raw/nse_shp_xbrl_pilot`, so a store of any other company held the index's promoter and public lines and no FPI, MF, insurance, holder or pledge row (all ten `tg_build/measure10` stores: 2 categories per quarter, 0 holder, 0 pledge rows). `_shp_parsed` now also looks up every file the profile companies' `shp_index` rows name, by name, in the pilot folder and then `raw/nse_shp_xbrl_universe` (T-209, every filing since 2024-04-01), on disk first, else on Drive through `raw_store` (manifest root `nse_shp_xbrl_universe`; one `prefetch` per build, the temp copy is deleted after parsing). `source_db` names the folder the file came from. A file held nowhere or unreadable is named on stderr and its quarter keeps the index rows; `REALITY_RAW_FETCH=0` keeps the build offline; tests never read the machine's manifest (autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py`). Copy of c01 (`--part stores` then `preferred`): +369 `shp_category` (10 quarters, 20-56 categories each), +130 `shp_holder`, +10 `shp_pledge`, +20 `share_capital`. The 20 rows `store_diff` lists as removed are the same 20 index rows with `pct_base_shares` and `pct_basis` now filled (pct unchanged), so the repair gate needs `--approve-loss` or `--accept`. Quarters before 2024-04 stay index-only (11 of 21 for c01): the universe fetch starts there. Tests: `python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_pilot3_shp_drive.py` -> 11 passed (under the dsh sandbox one of them needs a writable `tempfile` dir; the other 10 pass there). T-241 below replaced the whole-folder parse with a name-or-ISIN filter while keeping these rows.
- Tests: `python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_store_repair.py` -> 13 passed (fixture stores only, a fake
  step runner: stamps, stale reasons, baseline, rewrites, the skip rule, add-only kept, lost row refused and
  accepted, failed part restored, lock, notes).

### One view over every tracked item (T-219, 16 Nov 2026)

Shivam 16 Nov: tickets, ideas, rulings and store defects in four shapes become patchwork, and Claude must keep a
persistent memory of the work; trackers stay local, on Drive or in git/GitHub (Issues allowed), never a cloud
service. `reality_engine/scripts/items.py` reads all four sources into one item shape (type, id, status, title,
why, tags, links, source, opened, review_by): `tasks/tickets.jsonl` (written by `task_board`), `tasks/IDEA_BOX.md`,
`tasks/RULINGS.md` (one item per bullet, id `RUL-<section>-<n>`) and `tasks/store_repair/notes.jsonl` (written by
`store_repair note`). Links are the T-/I-/R-/H- ids an item's text names, shown with the linked item's status.
`items render` writes `tasks/ITEMS.md` and `reports/items.xlsx` (generated, never hand-edited); `items due` lists
ideas past their review date and open store defects; `items list [--type] [--open]`. Each source keeps its own
writer and its hooks, so nothing else changed. First render: 45 open tickets, 11 open ideas, 27 rulings, 4 open
defects. `items github [--apply]` mirrors every open ticket and open store defect to the repo's GitHub Issues:
`github_plan` is pure and keys one issue per item by the body marker `<!-- item:<id> -->`: create when missing
(labels `item:ticket` / `item:defect`), edit when the title or body drifted, reopening a closed issue whose item
is still open, close an open issue whose item is done or gone. Ideas and rulings are never mirrored; a dry run
prints the actions, `--apply` runs `gh` with argv lists (both labels first, no token read). Test:
`python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_items.py` -> 10 passed.

### Question bank (T-217, 16 Nov 2026)

`reality_engine/scripts/tg_questions.py` builds the investor-question bank for the Telegram queue: `harvest` runs one
agy job per company (`gemini-3.8-flash-medium`; each run in its own folder `D:/RealityEngine/tg_build/questions_raw/work/<isin>`, removed afterwards, logs in `tg_build/questions_logs`, because on 16 Nov one run deleted the shared log and fifteen other companies' raw files; only the company name
and exchange symbol are sent), `load` re-reads the raw JSON files and verifies the links, `check` passes a company at
>= 20 stored questions and >= 5 verified found, `export` writes the Excel workbook. The script, not the model, decides
`found` vs `found_unverified`: a `found` question keeps that kind only when its page loads and names the company; a
PDF on the company's own site is taken on its address, and so is an NSE archive file (`nsearchives.nseindia.com/corporate/<SYMBOL>_...`), which NSE names by the filer's symbol (16 Nov: 62 of 191 unverified questions in 4 companies were the companies' own call transcripts there; `load` re-verifies after a harvest); a site whose name (6+ letters) sits inside the symbol or the joined company name also counts (`autoaxle.com` for AUTOAXLES: 18 more). A PDF is first judged on the text layer of its first three pages (`pdf_text`, pypdf), so a BSE attachment named by a random id counts when it prints the company (30 questions, e.g. 'Scrip Name: BHAGERIA ... Transcript'); a scan with no text layer falls back to the address. Still unverified: YouTube links refused with 429 during the check, press and rating-agency PDFs that do not name the company. A `created` question without `grounding_fact` or
`data_needed` is dropped and counted, every second question per kind is frozen by hash (T-172), and `word_overlap` is
stored as a hand-read hint only. Trial on queue positions 1-3: 46/44/47 questions, 441-884 s and 190k-393k tokens per
company, 121/49/78 tool calls; position 3 had only 2 verified found questions because the model gave invalid YouTube
links, so the prompt was tightened. The bank (`questions.db`, the Excel export) lives on D:, and no company list is
written in the repo. Test: `python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_tg_questions.py` -> 7 passed (injected
fetcher and runner: queue, name tokens, validation, freezing, load/summarise, fenced JSON, one harvest run).

### Randomised question trials over the Telegram stores (16 Nov 2026)

Shivam's order (16 Nov): build all 91 queue stores, then trial 1, 2, ... until 90% right, fixing each gap with a
general script. (T-241, 23 Nov: `tg_measure_build --window-start 2026-04-01` builds a this-financial-year-only store; the universe list is every BSE filer since 2026-04-01, `D:/RealityEngine/tg_build/universe_fy27/universe_fy27.csv`.) `reality_engine/scripts/tg_trial.py sample --trial N` draws K questions per built company from
`questions.db` (default 2: one open, one frozen; `found_unverified` excluded; never a question an earlier trial
asked) into `D:/RealityEngine/tg_build/trials/trialN/questions.jsonl` plus `batch_XX.jsonl` (whole companies per
batch, one per answer worker); `--repeat M` also re-asks trial M's open questions to check the fixes. Workers follow
`tasks/handoff/tg_trial_answer_brief.md` (p3ask `--db <store>`, a `gap` field per answer) and write `answers/`;
a separate grader writes `grades/`; `merge` joins them into `graded.jsonl` (answer_run_report input) and prints the
right-rate on frozen and open questions apart. Only the frozen rate is the reported score. Test:
`python -m pytest -q reality_engine/tests/test_tg_trial.py` -> 5 passed.

`tg_trial check --answers --calls --batch [--min-kinds 3] [--prev <first pass>]` lists the qids whose answer
breaks the brief: no answer, no logged p3ask call, `partly`/`not_found` after fewer than `min-kinds` different
tools, or (with `--prev`) a `partly`/`not_found` answer unchanged from an earlier pass. The answer brief makes the
worker run it before finishing. `merge` applies the 9 Nov ruling: a `partly`/`not_held` grade whose root cause is
`question` (the grader's own search confirms no filing states it) counts as right; `grade_strict` keeps the grader's
grade, and both rates are printed; `scope_out` questions are not scored.

Trial 1 (17 Nov, 182 questions): beam-free answerers gave up after one search (30 of 44 in one batch) and, sent
back, logged extra calls while keeping every answer word for word, so answering and grading run on DeepSeek
(`tasks/handoff/tg_trial1/`). Score: frozen 52/87 right (60%; strict 49%), open 54/88 (61%). Non-right causes:
raw_missing 28, question 23, not_extracted 21, reading 12, wrong_row 3, routing 3, compute 2. The fixes are store
repair notes R-014 (calls/decks not held: 27 stores hold neither), R-015 (AR statements and notes not extracted),
R-016 (segment notes and sector KPIs), R-017 (wrong kpi rows: column and Mn/crore), R-018 (9 stores without
shareholding). Report `D:/RealityEngine/reports/2026-11-17_tg_trial1.xlsx`.

## Answer arms, round 13 (T-205, 13 Nov 2026)

Shivam's order: the seeded tool run FIRST, then the round-12 pack path answered by the same model, graded blind
together with the earlier sets. Model route: `gemini-4-hydrogen-high` answers and `claude-opus-5-5-medium` grades,
both through the `agy` CLI; DeepSeek builds and smoke-tests only, Claude launches the full runs.

**The two arms.**

* **Arm P (pack), `reality_engine/answer/answer_step.py`.** The round-12 path unchanged: one round-11 reading copy
  per question, ONE model call per question plus at most one repair, the script's calc evaluator, the figure/citation
  check and the first-ten gate. Only the transport changes: `--answer-model agy:gemini-4-hydrogen-high` routes the
  same bytes through `reality_engine/answer/agy_call.py`, and each `answer_<qid>.json` gains `model_tools` and
  `model_data_tool_calls` so the record says whether the model left the pack for the store (it must not: the pack is
  the evidence). `pack_recall.py --plan-model agy:<id>` likewise routes the plan step through `agy_call`; a paid
  direct model is still refused by `plan_model.write_plan`.
* **Arm S (seeded tool run), `reality_engine/answer/seeded_run.py`.** Per question ONE agy Hydrogen run whose prompt
  is the T-199 answer rules rewritten for the `pilot3_*` MCP tools (`pilot3_fact` first for a single number, then
  `fin`/`ratio`/`kpi`/`guidance`/`statements`/`topic`/`cells`/`docs`/`search`/`semantic`/`holding`/`tech`/`sql`, every
  call with `symbol` and `as_of`, arithmetic through `pilot3_calc`, always `as_of`), plus the question, its as_of,
  and the same pack's reading copy labelled "evidence already gathered: start from it, check it, fill what is missing
  with tools". The model answers one JSON object (`answer`, `sources`, `confidence`); the tools in the record come
  from the agy event log, never from the model's claim. `answers.jsonl` is the ds_a shape (qid, question, as_of,
  answer, tools, sources, sql_reason, confidence) with `tool_calls`, `tools_by_name`, `parsed`, `model`, `seconds`,
  `ok`, `error`; `calls.jsonl` holds one line per agy run (attempts, tokens, tool calls by tool, log path).
  Resumable: a qid already in `answers.jsonl` is skipped.

**The agy route (`agy_call.run_agy`).** `agy -p <prompt> --model <id> --mode accept-edits --output-format
stream-json --print-timeout <n>s --disable-slash-commands --dangerously-skip-permissions`, no shell, stdout to the
event log and `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` (MSI rule). The event log is parsed by the agy-worker's own
``parse_events` in plugins/agy-worker/server/agy_core.py` (one parser in the repo). One retry on a transport error (no final
response, the process failed to start); a run that produced a final answer is NEVER retried. Windows caps a command
line at 32,767 characters while a pack reading copy is ~75,000, so a prompt over 24,000 characters is written to
`<cwd>/.tmp/agy_prompts/prompt_<stamp>_<chars>.txt` and the argv carries a one-line pointer that the model reads
first (`read_file`/`view_file`); that read is the only tool call arm P shows. Tests use a fake agy script passed as an
argv list — the real binary is never called in tests.

**The commands Claude runs (full 64 questions).** From the repo root, each run writes its own directory:

```
python -m reality_engine.answer.seeded_run --set bluestar --packs D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/pack_r11 \
    --out .tmp/answers_r13/arm_s --workspace .                      # arm S FIRST (Shivam's order)
python -m reality_engine.answer.answer_step --set bluestar --packs D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/pack_r11 \
    --out .tmp/answers_r13/arm_p --answer-model agy:gemini-4-hydrogen-high --workspace .
python -m reality_engine.answer.blind_grade make --set pack=.tmp/answers_r13/arm_p \
    --set seeded=.tmp/answers_r13/arm_s --set ds_a=D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/answers_ds_a \
    --set ds_b=D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/answers_ds_b --out D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/report_r13 \
    --questions D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/answers/questions_in.json --batch 8
python -m reality_engine.answer.blind_grade run  --out D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/report_r13 --workspace .
python -m reality_engine.answer.blind_grade decode --out D:/RealityEngine/bluestar/report_r13
```

`make` shuffles the labels per question with `--seed` (default 20261114), writes `grade_in_<n>.jsonl` (8 questions
each), `GRADING.md` and the key at `<out>/../key_private/key_<out.name>.csv`; the grader is told never to open it.
`run` is one agy `claude-opus-5-5-medium` call per batch and accepts ONE JSON line per (qid, label): `qid`, `label`,
`grade` (`right`/`partly`/`wrong`/`not_held`), `root_cause`, `truth`, `note`; strict parsing, one re-ask when a batch
does not parse, and the missing lines are reported in `grade_run.json` (`unparsed`). `decode` writes `decoded.json`
and `per_question.csv`: per-set totals (`not_held` counts as right, ruling 9 Nov), the per-question table, and
pairwise agreement (`agree_grade` on the strict grade, `agree_right` on right-or-not_held). The grade rules are
GRADING_1.md with the shell's `p3ask` swapped for the same `pilot3_*` MCP tools, because the agy grader has no shell;
`--rules <file>` replaces them.

**Round-13 smoke (13 Nov, BS-001..BS-003, agy runs in the worker).** Arm P: 3/3 answered, 8 figures quoted, 0
data-tool calls, 3 model calls, 204,091 input / 22,898 output tokens, median 52.85 s. Arm S: 3/3 answered, 47 tool
calls, 0 unparsed, 233 s total. One Opus batch: 6/6 grades parsed, both sets 3/3 right, 3/3 agreement. Files under
`.tmp/r13_smoke/`. Stop rule checked before building: agy runs in the sandbox and `pilot3_fact` resolves BLUESTARCO to
`D:\RealityEngine\bluestar\bluestar.db` through the store registry. Tests: `python -m pytest plugins/agy-worker/tests
reality_engine/tests/test_answer_step.py reality_engine/tests/test_plan_model.py reality_engine/tests/test_agy_call.py
reality_engine/tests/test_seeded_run.py reality_engine/tests/test_blind_grade.py -q` (63 passed, 1 skipped).

## Answer checker, round 15 (T-213, 14 Nov 2026)

Shivam kept run B (the DeepSeek agent with `p3ask`, unlimited calls, whole pages: 54/64) as the
baseline and asked for a code checker around it: no model call inside the checker, no
per-question rule, no evidence pack, and a follow-up text built only from what the code found.
`reality_engine/answer/answer_checker.py` reads one answer set (`answers.jsonl` + `calls.jsonl`,
the T-199 shape) and one store, and reports five checks per answer. A repair agent
(`tasks/handoff/T-213_R15_REPAIR_BRIEF.md`, run by Claude) then rewrites only the flagged lines
and re-runs the checker (max 2 rounds).

**The five checks.** `C1` look-ahead: every cited source and every document named in words
("FY26 annual report", "Q2FY27 call") is known at or before `as_of`; the fact names the late
document and its `known_at`. `C2` calls covered: a question asking how something changed
(change/across/revised/trend **and** calls, management, guidance or commentary) must cite every
transcript whose call falls in the window the question names, or the last four calls; the fact
lists the missing calls by period, `doc_key` and date. `C3` speaker: a sentence attributing
figures to management must have each figure on a cited transcript page under a management
speaker label (role `md`/`mgmt`; `md` is the Managing Director's row, `mgmt` the CFO's, so both
are management); when the figure sits under another label, or a named call is not cited, the
fact names the speaker/page. Hyphenated ranges ("Rs 12-13 crore") are matched as a range, not
as two integers. `C4` scope: a segment question must carry a scope word in the answer, and the
segment's own printed figure for the same metric and date — found in `doc_page` with a 2-gram
match on the question's metric words and the question's date/quarter — must be the value used;
the fact prints the segment figure, its document and page. `C5` dug before "not found": a
not-found/not-stated part needs at least three of search, semantic, topic, cells and statements,
one `page` read, and four distinct lookup tools in `calls.jsonl`.

**CLI and output.**
```
python -m reality_engine.answer.answer_checker --db <store> --answers <folder> --out <file.jsonl> [--qid BS-031 ...]
```
One output line per answer: `{"qid", "checks": [{"id", "ok", "fact"}], "followup"}`, where
`followup` is empty or `Check failed: C3 - <fact>. Look again in <doc list>.` for each failed
check. The checker never opens the grades; grades are used only to measure it.

**Measured on run B (14 Nov, `answers_ds_b`, `.tmp/r15/check_ds_b.jsonl`).** 64 answers,
15 flagged. Of the 10 graded misses (1 wrong, 9 partly) it flags 9: BS-008 and BS-040 (C4, the
company-wide 7,229.22 cr order book used for the segment's 5,141.78 cr), BS-018 (C3, the
analyst's Rs 12-13 cr given to management), BS-027 and BS-034 (C3, figures under a non-management
speaker or a named call not cited), BS-026 and BS-051 (C5, not-found after three routes),
BS-031 (C1, the FY26 annual report at an as_of of 2024-11-30) and BS-036 (C2, one of six calls
cited). It does not flag BS-025 (prose wording the checks cannot see). It flags 6 of the 54
non-miss answers — the three `not_held` answers (BS-009, BS-055, BS-059, which did stop early)
and three right answers whose management figure sat off the cited page (BS-016, BS-017,
BS-019); the target was >=8 of 10 misses and <=8 false alarms.

**Tests.** `python -m pytest reality_engine/tests/test_answer_checker.py -q` (10 tests, 10
passed): a tmp store built from a few real Blue Star rows, one failing-then-passing test per
check on the run-B cases (BS-031, BS-034, BS-018, BS-008, BS-026), a check that a
figure-change-between-quarters question is not a calls question, and a CLI test. The tests build
their case directory under `.tmp/answer_checker_tests/` because pytest's own `tmp_path` cannot
scandir under the Windows sandbox; `--basetemp` is not needed.

## Evidence pack (T-205)

Two planners live in `reality_engine/answer/`. `--planner script` is the round-3 word-matching plan. `--planner model` builds a per-company menu (`menu.build`, read-only, `menu.render` under 8,000 chars: financial lines by table with annual-only cash-flow/balance-sheet lines, KPI/ratio names with period ranges, guidance metrics with call dates, holding categories and quarter ends, the price range and `price_rows`, calls and decks by doc key/date/pages, other doc types as counts) and asks ONE free Beam call (`beam-free`, `--plan-model`) for the question's `parts` and at most 25 `{part, route, args}`; `plan_model.validate` checks every route (`routes.yaml` `plan_routes`), argument and menu name, one repair call fixes what it can, rejected entries go to `route_errors`, and invalid JSON after the repair falls back to the script planner (`planner: "script_fallback"`). `gather(..., lookups=, parts=)` is a UNION: the round-3 script routes run at full quota AND the plan lookups run, each with its own per-route quota; a planned lookup that returns nothing is retried by script (without its period, then each significant word of `terms`/`query` alone, logged under the lookup's `retries`); every planned `fin`/`fact` metric+period also fetches the printed `cells` for it (`derived`); every `search`/`statements` hit in a call or deck reads the full page of its top 3 hits (`derived`); `lookups_extra.price_rows` serves price sessions with the previous session's close, adjusted close and a first-to-last adjusted return (prior close, multi-year return; a range over 80 sessions returns its two endpoints and the return row, never 1,500 sessions), and holding lookups add the filed share count at each quarter end asked. Coverage is per part with an explicit all/some/none `status`, each lookup's items, retries and derived lookups; a lookup or retry with 0 items prints `not found` and counts as not found. The pack keeps plan-lookup items first. The reading copy (`pack_<qid>.txt`, the scored and answered text) is the round-6 rendering with a **150,000-character cap**: a typed row prints its lead line plus the row content (the `quote` and every numeric field), page/statement text prints whole, an identical item prints once, and the only trimming is a row's provenance blob, absolute paths and null/empty fields. Over 150k only script page/rest items are dropped from the lowest rank up (logged in `reading_cut`); typed rows and plan items are never dropped. Compaction to 60,000 characters was tried in rounds 7-8 and lost evidence (reading-copy hit/partial/miss 34/20/10 and 27/31/11 in round 6 -> 27/26/11 and 26/30/13 in round 8): do not repeat it. The score is the reading copy's bytes (`hit`), with `hit_json` and `hit_rounded` beside it; `hit_clean` was removed because it misclassified real figures. `pack_recall._strip_noise` ignores an item's lead label, so the label is not evidence: rescored with that rule the round-9 and the round-11 copies both score 36/20/8 (Blue Star) and 30/29/10 (Aurobindo); the older 37 included BS-017's truth number 116 matching the positional label `[116]`, which the copy's text does not carry. Kept: `event_doc` price rows, 0 items = NOT FOUND, every part needs a lookup, and the shared `lookups_tech.day0_for` rule. Each question writes `pack_<qid>.json`, `pack_<qid>.txt` and `plan_<qid>.json` (validated plan + raw replies); plans cache in `.tmp/plan_cache/` by (question, as_of, menu text, model, rules version). Run: `python -m reality_engine.answer.pack_recall --set bluestar --workspace . --planner model --out .tmp/pack_r11/bluestar` (or `aurobindo`; summary in `summary.json`, `--limit N` for a smoke run). The semantic route needs `REALITY_DATA_ALLOW_PAID_EMBED=1`. Tests: `python -m pytest reality_engine/tests/test_evidence_pack.py reality_engine/tests/test_plan_model.py -q --basetemp=.tmp/pt1` (the model call is mocked; the round-5 tests cover the union, retries, cells-for-fin and price_rows).

## Answer step (T-205 round 11)

`python -m reality_engine.answer.answer_step --set bluestar --packs .tmp/pack_r11/bluestar --out .tmp/answers_r11/bluestar --answer-model deepseek-v4.1-flash --retry-unparseable --reuse .tmp/answers_r10/bluestar --reuse-packs .tmp/pack_r9/bluestar` reads one round-11 reading copy per question and makes ONE model call (stable Go session id). The default is the approved paid `deepseek-v4.1-flash` (Shivam, 13 Nov, while Beam is rate limited); `beam-free` is still selectable. A 429 waits 30, 60, 120 then 240 seconds; a question still limited is written with `status: "rate_limited"`, a finished answer is skipped on resume, `--retry-unparseable` re-asks the unparseable ones, and `--reuse DIR` reuses a round-10 saved reply that passes the new check offline (`--reuse-packs`, default `.tmp/pack_r9/<set>`, supplies the old copy's printed order for its positional ids), so a fixed check need not re-buy the answers. Files: `reality_engine/answer/answer_step.py` (this section), `reality_engine/answer/evidence_pack.py` (`_reading_block` prints the id, `reading_order` exposes the printed order) and `reality_engine/answer/pack_recall.py` (`_strip_noise` ignores the lead label).

Round 10's defect: the reading copy printed a position (`[1] fin ...`) while the check wanted the pack id (`fin-3`); the model copied `fin-1`, `12` or `[21]`, so 108 of 119 Blue Star figures cited an id the copy never printed, and the offline re-check recovered 96 of them by mapping the old positions. The round-11 copy prints each item's pack id as the only label (`[fin-3] doc=... p...`); `check_answer()` resolves exactly that id, tolerant of brackets, case and surrounding spaces, and nothing else. A figure's quote must appear in its cited item after whitespace/line-break normalisation; a quote that still differs falls back to the value appearing in that item's text (unit and printed-rounded forms allowed; a bare 4-digit integer in the item is matched even though it is a year elsewhere), kept as `verified_value_only` and flagged, never removed; a value that is not in the cited item is still removed. Dates (30 September 2026, 31.03.2026), fiscal-period labels (Q2FY27, H1, FY2025-26, 52-week), page references (p. 9), ordinals and labelled references (1st, Segment 1, Table 2, Tier-3), years and counts restating the question are not figures, so they are neither checked nor scrubbed; everything else is.

The model never computes: `compute()` evaluates each calc expression with an AST arithmetic-only evaluator, fills `{{name}}` placeholders and keeps the formula with its inputs beside the answer. Round 10 also dropped the calcs the model wrote INSIDE a part (the JSON skeleton has `calcs` per part) because the normaliser read only a top-level key: 16 Blue Star answers kept raw `{{...}}` text. `_normalise` now collects per-part calcs, remaps the part-relative input indices onto the flattened figures list, and a calc whose input figure did not pass the citation check fails (`did not pass the check`), so a removed figure cannot leak back through a computation; a placeholder with no calculation becomes `[unverified]` and is listed. Every number of the final answer must be a checked figure or a computed result. Failures go back in ONE repair call, and whatever still fails is removed and listed under `unverified` — never silently kept. Empty or length-cut replies are retried once; `MAX_TOKENS` is 8,000 (the longest valid round-10 reply was 2,034 output tokens). The runner's first-ten gate stops a run when more than 20% of cited figures were removed, printing the reasons. A part is `not_found` only with coverage status none or it states what it read (`abstained_with_evidence`).

`--recheck` re-runs compute + check on the saved `raw_replies` with no model call (old positional ids map through the old reading copy's printed order; figures whose id cannot be mapped are reported and counted as removed): round 10 Blue Star 96 of 119 figures were saved by the id fix, 42 of 52 parseable answers had nothing removed (90 verified, 7 `verified_value_only`, 22 removed: 16 quote+value, 5 unmapped id, 1 value); Aurobindo 88 of 130 and 50 of 66 (92 / 10 / 28: 11 quote+value, 14 unmapped id, 3 value). `--refill` replays the same replies and rewrites the answers (calcs, placeholders, scrub) with no model call. Round-11 Blue Star result: 64/64 answers, 0 unparseable, 199 figures cited, 165 quote-verified, 34 `verified_value_only`, 0 removed, 64 answers with nothing removed, 98 parts answered / 17 not_found / 9 abstained, 27 calcs, 35 answers reused; 39 model calls (including the first-ten gate run), 1,355,853 input and 153,423 output tokens, 901.2 s of model time (max 84.6 s). Answers cache in `.tmp/answer_cache/` by (reading-copy hash, model, prompt version); each question writes `answer_<qid>.json` plus one `answers.csv`/`summary.json`, and `recheck.json`/`refill.json` beside them. Tests (model mocked): `python -m pytest reality_engine/tests/test_answer_step.py reality_engine/tests/test_evidence_pack.py reality_engine/tests/test_plan_model.py -q --basetemp=.tmp/pt1` (50 passed). Round 12 formats figure and result tokens in the text by script (integers without `.0`, thousands separators, rupee amounts of one crore or more as crore at two decimals, percentages at two decimals with no space before `%`, a percentage difference labelled `percentage points` once), names the calc, the unresolved input and the figures that do exist in the repair, and after repair replaces only that result with `not computed (input missing: <name>)` while flagging the part; six answers were re-asked and the rest refilled offline into `.tmp/answers_r12/bluestar` (200 figures cited: 165 quoted, 35 `verified_value_only`, 0 removed; 29 calcs; round-12 cost 9 model calls, 269,098 input and 27,562 output tokens) — 52 tests pass.
- **Quota pacing (`scripts/quota_pace.py`, T-242, Shivam 24 Nov 2026).** Shivam: a full 5-hour window costs ~8-9% of
  the weekly quota, so unspent windows waste the weekly reset. `python -m reality_engine.scripts.quota_pace` prints,
  per account in `ops_rules.json` "quota": windows left to the deadline (rest of the current one plus every 5 h after
  its reset), the most a full spend of each reaches, and the share of EVERY window to spend
  (`(target - weekly) / (k * windows)`), with what the current window should show by now. `k` (weekly points per full
  window) starts at 8.5 and is learned from `.tmp/quota_samples.jsonl` (weekly growth / 5-hour growth inside one
  window). The ops gate flags an account whose 5-hour use is >10 points behind that pace.
- **Deck guards (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Wolf; deck pilot grade `D:/RealityEngine/t242/deck_grade/deck_grade.md`: verified
  31/7/2/3 of 43).** `build_observations(deck_guards=True)` (set for doc_type deck): a growth/change measure printed
  after a decrease word in its own clause (`DECREASE_RE`: decreased, declined, de-growth, negative growth, fell, lower by...) is negative
  ("EBITDA decreased 79%" was +79); a page-1/2 filing cover letter gives no rows. On deck and v2 transcript rows a NEW:
  measure with no unit is `no_unit` (unverified): "Regulation 30", "since 2021" and unlabelled Rs Cr tables
  were verified. (Printed ranges and date-shaped values: the deck value-ranges entry at the end of this file.)
  Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_deck_guards.py`.
- **Deck entity guard (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Wolf).** Strict deck grade (`D:/RealityEngine/t242/deck_grade2/deck_grade2_strict.md`,
  verified 48/5/2/5): the reader already tags SIAM production and a market size `industry` and government capex
  `country`, but the script verified them. With `deck_guards`, a row whose `entity_scope` is industry, country, peer or
  customer is `not_company` (served labelled, never verified). Another company's statement inside a deck (Ambuja's
  balance sheet in ACC's deck) and an unnamed subsidiary's margin are still tagged `company` by the reader: prompt work.
  Grader warning: some decks print the value BEFORE its label (BLACKROSE p, 4 of 60 pages); grade by the arithmetic.

- **Deck page widening, two options (`reality_engine/v2/observations.py`, `pages_from_store`/`process_page`, T-242,
  25 Nov 2026, DeepSeek).** Both add deck pages the router today sends nowhere, both decide on the stored `reason`
  text in `routes.csv` (never rewritten), and both are off by default on their own:
  **`--deck-few-figures`** (`deck_few_figures`) also reads a deck page routed `no_figures` whose reason prints
  `N printed figures (fewer than 4)` with N in 1-3 (`FEW_FIGURES_REASON`): +2,307 pages on the 677 universe stores
  that hold a routes.csv, on top of the 4,683 `table_or_chart` deck pages read today.
  **`--deck-jumbled-min SHARE`** (`deck_jumbled_min`, float, 0 = off) also reads a deck `needs_ocr` page whose reason
  prints `text layer jumbled: label-dictionary share X` (`JUMBLED_SHARE_REASON`, `jumbled_share()`) with X >= SHARE,
  straight from its own text layer, no OCR words needed: +1,438 pages at 0.70, +2,483 at 0.65, +3,240 at 0.60 (the
  whole ladder, per-option samples in `tasks/t242_deck_widening_measure.md`).
  `process_page(..., text_source=...)` marks every row that verified against that layer
  `text_source='text_layer_jumbled'` (a page whose OCR words exist still reads on OCR: `ocr_read` wins and the marker
  is not applied); `evaluate_status` keeps the marker through `--recheck`. `run()` passes the marker only for a page
  that carries one, so the old five-argument `process_page` monkeypatch in tests still matches. The deck prompt is
  unchanged (`read_gate` fingerprint `ea5e621d2826`). Counts, the SHARE ladder and the 20 random additions per option
  (seed 21, first 300 characters of text) are in `tasks/t242_deck_widening_measure.md`, which also carries the
  no-model-call snippet that reproduces the counts. Tests in
  `reality_engine/tests/test_observations.py`: `test_deck_few_figures_option_reads_only_the_one_to_three_figure_pages`,
  `test_deck_jumbled_min_reads_only_the_pages_at_or_above_the_share`,
  `test_a_jumbled_deck_page_read_records_text_layer_jumbled_on_its_rows`,
  `test_run_forwards_the_widening_options_and_the_jumbled_marker`.
- **Annual-report useful sections (`v2/ar_sections.py`, T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Wolf).** `kept_pages(pages)` picks the AR
  table pages worth a hy4 read: MD&A, highlights, segment, operations (order book, capacity), subsidiaries (AOC-1),
  off-balance notes (contingent liabilities, related parties, borrowing terms, ageing, revenue split), ratios,
  disputes, material RPTs; a heading-zone drop section (audit, governance, notice, CSR/BRSR, primary statements,
  policies) wins; an unheaded page after a running kept section continues it. Measured on 120 universe stores: 3,309
  of 14,743 table pages kept (22%). Wired into `observations.pages_from_store`, `observations.run`, and the CLI via
  `--ar-sections` (off by default, `ar_sections=False`), filtering only `annual_report` pages and counting dropped
  pages under `dropped["ar_section"]` while leaving other document types untouched.
- **Annual-report selector round 2 (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Wolf).** A hand audit of 80 pages (Sonnet, read-only,
  `D:/RealityEngine/t242/ar_audit/ar_audit.csv`) found the round-1 keep-list 42% precise with about half the useful
  pages dropped. `ar_sections` is now drop-list first: a heading-zone boilerplate section (audit, governance, notice,
  CSR/BRSR, employee benefits, primary statements, policies, and the XBRL note schedules: PPE, share capital, other
  equity, deferred tax, payables, inventories, EPS...) drops the page, except that a notes running header skips the
  primary-statement/policies/CSR drops (Eagle); keep headings for off-balance notes, material RPTs and subsidiaries need
  a figure table (`numeric_table`: >= 8 lines with amounts); any other figure table is kept as `table`; prose is
  dropped. `needs_ocr` pages have empty text and are dropped unless they continue a kept MD&A/risks/highlights section.
  120 universe stores: 8,055 of 14,743 table pages kept (55%). Re-audit of precision and recall before the AR pilot.
- **Annual-report selector round 3: safe drops + hy4 triage (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Wolf).** The round-2 re-audit
  (`D:/RealityEngine/t242/ar_audit2/ar_audit2.md`) found the figure-table fallback 15% precise. Both audits (160 pages,
  `D:/RealityEngine/t242/ar_gold/gold.json`) now score any selector offline (`eval.py <module>`): round 1 39% precise /
  44% recall, round 2 27% / 81%, hy4 text-only triage 67% / 100% (`hy4_classify.py`, 198k input tokens for 160 pages).
  `--ar-sections` therefore drops only `SAFE_DROP` headings (notice, governance, audit, CSR/BRSR, remuneration, market
  data, cover letters, policies; 30 of 160 gold pages, no useful one) and empty pages, then asks hy4 `TRIAGE_PROMPT`
  per page; verdicts are cached in `C:/RealityEngine/t242/ar_triage.db`; an unparsed reply counts as useful and is
  not cached. Eagle's review: the triage runs in `run()` on the SAMPLED pages only (`triage_selection`: the seeded order, topped up until `--limit` useful pages, at most `TRIAGE_OVERSAMPLE`=3 x limit questions, after `read_gate.check` of `limit` reads), and `TRIAGE_PROMPT` is part of `read_gate.fingerprint('annual_report')`; `needs_ocr` AR pages have no text and are safe-dropped until their OCR words exist. The gold set wrote the triage definition, so a fresh hand audit of triage verdicts gates the AR bulk.
- **Transcript statements gate (T-242, Shivam 24 Nov 2026).** Strict 60-row grades on `394_v2_reparsed4.db`: verified
  call NUMBERS 35/15/7/3 (58% right, 17% wrong+junk; five guard rounds 52-68%), verbatim STATEMENTS 47/11/0/2 (78%, 3%):
  `D:/RealityEngine/t242/grades/reparse4/` and `reparse4_statements/`. Shivam chose: transcripts gate on statements;
  `apply_transcript_number_gate()` turns every verified transcript number/guidance row unverified
  (`transcript_number:` reason) after `build_observations` in both the read path and `--reparse`. The guards still run
  (their tests check the page check). A call number earns verified only from a filing we hold (results XBRL or a
  verified deck row, same company, measure and period within 1%): Eagle's corroboration matcher, separate step.
- **Call-number corroboration (`v2/corroborate.py`, tests `reality_engine/tests/test_corroborate.py`, T-242,
  24 Nov 2026, DeepSeek).** The other half of the transcript
  gate. A call NUMBER earns `verified` from a store row with the same symbol and normalised period (`Q2 FY27` ->
  `Q2FY27`), a compatible basis (a stated basis is required; an empty one takes consolidated then standalone, and the
  citation names it) and `value*scale` within 1% (0.05 absolute for percentages; with `value_high`, any filed value
  inside the printed range). `NUMBER_MAP` sends revenue_from_operations/pat to `fin_quarter_xbrl` then `ratio_panel`,
  pbt/total_income to the XBRL lines, ebitda and the `*_pct` margin/growth rows to `ratio_panel` (EBITDA has no XBRL
  line), and order.book/order.intake/capex/capacity/volume to `kpi_series`; a measure with no filed counterpart is
  counted and left alone. A GUIDANCE row matches the store's `guidance` row (same metric and target period, `said_on`
  within 120 days, low/high within 1%). A filed row is `pre_call` when its `known_at` is at most one day after the
  call's, else `post_call` (still a citation, it says the filing came later); unit families never cross, so a USD or
  tonne filing never corroborates a rupee number. The CLI copies `--obs` (never edits it) and rewrites only the
  matched rows' reason to `corroborated: <table>.<metric> <period> <basis> = <value> <unit> (<pre|post>_call)`:
  `python -m reality_engine.v2.corroborate --obs <db> --stores-root C:/RealityEngine/tg_build/full394 --out
  C:/RealityEngine/t242/394_v2_corroborated.db [--limit-symbols N]`. Measured 24 Nov on `394_v2_reparsed4.db`
  (350 stores, 262 symbols): 10,543 call rows considered, 352 corroborated (327 `pre_call` / 25 `post_call`;
  `fin_quarter_xbrl` 154, `ratio_panel` 146, `guidance` 52), 1,938 number rows with a counterpart unmatched, 7,932
  rows with no filed counterpart at all (6,648 number, 1,284 guidance; 2,874 distinct number measures), 407 empty
  period, ~7 s; the input DB is byte-identical after the run.
- **Quota handoff rule is per account (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, Wolf).** `ops_gate.quota_lines` raises "5-hour window at N%
  - hand work to..." only in the session whose `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (default `~/.claude`) is that account's config: the
  manager that received the work no longer gets blocked by the other account's full window until its reset.
- **Annual-report statement gate (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, `v2/observations.py`).** Strict grade of `pilot_ar_fy27.db`
  (60 rows, `D:/RealityEngine/t242/ar_grade/ar_grade.md`): verified R/P/W/J 42/8/10/0 -- every wrong row that mapped
  to a statement line was a SUBSET of the line or a wrong column (one bank balance as cash and cash equivalents,
  USD-only trade payables as the total, a deferred-tax line as inventories, a director's AOC-2 dividend as dividend
  paid, segment allocated expenses as total expenses). Shivam 8 Nov: "XBRL stays as filed", so an AR page never
  verifies a statement line. `apply_ar_statement_gate()` turns every verified NUMBER row whose measure is a
  statement line unverified (`ar_statement_line:` reason); the row stays served, labelled. `STATEMENT_LINE` is
  derived from the vocabulary's own driver-tree node (the fourth `MEASURES` field): a money / money_per_unit measure
  on a balance-sheet, P&L or cash-flow node -- 133 ids, `STATEMENT_NODES`; six reviewed operating measures on those
  nodes keep every check (`OPERATING_MONEY_MEASURES`: capex.estimated, crude_oil_price, ebitda_per_kg,
  ebitda_per_tonne, realestate.presales, share_price), and an operating measure (capacity, volumes, order book,
  employees, segment KPIs) never matches. A declared `NEW:<id>` has no node, so a statement word in its name
  (`NEW_STATEMENT_WORDS`: assets, liabilities, payables, receivables, inventories, expenses, income, revenue, sale,
  cash, provisions, dividend, depreciation, eps, ...) marks it a statement line -- "equity" and "tax" alone are
  deliberately absent (a shareholding or a tax-dispute disclosure is not one). Wired by `apply_number_gates(rows,
  doc_type, page_text=...)` in the read path (`process_page`), in `--reparse` and in `--recheck` (the four row gates in
  the next entry run from the same call); `--reparse` now also rebuilds `annual_report`
  pages from `page_reply` with the reader's own inputs (text layer, words, deck as-of/basis, stored OCR text), and
  `--recheck` re-applies the gate so a recheck never re-verifies one. With `--reparse`, `--stores-root` means EVERY
  `cNN/cNN.db` under the root (Eagle, 24 Nov: it used to take the `--sample 5` seeded stores, so 197 of 200 AR
  pages came back `no_store`). Measured on a copy of
  `D:/RealityEngine/hy4_out/pilot_ar_fy27.db` (200 pages, 5,607 rows) with `--reparse`: verified 3,017 -> 1,124
  (1,878 rows gated; the rest of the change is re-parse input drift). Of the 60 graded rows: 9 of 10 W now
  unverified (the tenth, AVL `NEW:loyalty_rewards_given_away`, is a period error on prose, not a statement line),
  24 R and 2 P lost verified (all statement lines; XBRL is their authority), 25 rows stay verified -- W+J 1/25 = 4%
  (bar <= 5%). Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_ar_sections.py`; the transcript gate and its fingerprint
  (`e95108f16e65`) are untouched.

- **Transcript statement speakers (`v2/obs_speaker.py` `attach_statement_speakers`, T-242, 24 Nov 2026, DeepSeek).**
  The strict 60-row statement grade (`D:/RealityEngine/t242/grades/reparse4_statements/`) failed only on attribution:
  a null speaker on a page that opens mid-answer (SATIN p4, UNOMINDA p6, HINDZINC p5), the company name as speaker
  (ASHOKA "Ashoka Buildcon Limited", BHAGYANGR), a first name or initials alone (ANURAS "Gopal", ANGELONE "AK"), a
  reply with no question ("Yes, the model is similar.", TVSSCS) and a page-1 Reg 30 cover letter stored as a
  statement (ADANIPORTS p1; measured here on UNOMINDA p1 and KAMATHOTEL p1). Script-level fix, no prompt change:
  `attach_statement_speakers(rows, pages, symbol,
  company_names)` runs after `build_observations` in `reality_engine/v2/observations.py` (the `process_page` path and
  `--reparse`). A statement with no speaker is re-attributed by `attribute_document`: the last label before it on
  the page, else the last label of an earlier page, and a carried analyst/moderator label is refused. A speaker
  that is the company name (legal-form words stripped, so "ASHOKA" equals "Ashoka Buildcon Limited") is not a
  person: it is left as printed and the row goes unverified (`speaker_unresolved:`); a company label is never
  re-attributed to a nearby analyst label (BHAGYANGR p12 prints the company as the speaker beside an analyst's
  questions; the first cut borrowed that analyst's name, so the rule was tightened). What remains is resolved against the
  doc's management list (`management_pool`: the parsed roster plus turns carrying a management title): a single
  first name/surname or initials resolves when exactly one name matches, else the label is kept and the row is
  unverified `speaker_unresolved:`; a statement still without a label is unverified `speaker_unresolved: no speaker
  label...`; a statement under 8 words opening yes/no/right/correct/sure is unverified `no_referent:`.
  **Widening that guard (T-242, 25 Nov 2026) was built, measured and reverted -- not merged.** Five structural shapes
  (lowercase start = mid-sentence, a bare ordinal whose list is outside, a short reply whose topic is only a pronoun
  or a demonstrative, a short passage whose only figure is a percentage with no metric, plus the yes/no rule) flag all
  8 P rows of the fy27_v3 ckpt_0003 grade, but on that DB they turn 1,984 of 15,517 verified statements unverified
  (12.79%); the cheapest set that still covers all 8 graded rows is 5.99%, over the brief's 5% stop bar, so nothing
  shipped. Per-rule counts, the 30-row seed-5 sample and the three options (raise the bar / high-precision shapes only
  / gate at the answer layer) are in `tasks/t242_no_referent_v3_measure.md`.
  `build_doc_context` now skips a page-1 filing cover letter (its "Reference:" line read as a speaker and killed the
  roster), `is_cover_letter` needs 2 of 3 marks (scrip/BSE code/trading symbol/listing department/Regulation 30,
  "Sub.:", "Dear Sir/Madam") so a "BSE Scrip:" transcript cover (UNOMINDA) and a "Sub.:" Reg 30 intimation
  (KAMATHOTEL, ADANIPORTS) give no rows, and section headings/discourse markers ("Asset Quality", "Mobility",
  "First", "Finally") joined `_NON_SPEAKER_WORDS` after run 5 carried them as speakers. Measured on a copy of
  `C:/RealityEngine/t242/394_v2_reparsed4.db` with `--reparse --stores-root C:/RealityEngine/tg_build/full394`
  (2,000 pages, 0 `no_store`): statement rows verified 5,719 -> 5,456, unverified 3,029 -> 3,233, quarantined
  556 -> 546; statements with an empty speaker 1,628 -> 319 (17.5% -> 3.5%; none of the 319 is verified); 1,305
  speakers carried, 73 resolved to a full management name, 432 rows carry a `speaker_unresolved:` reason and 52 a
  `no_referent:` one. Of the 60 graded rows 46 of the 47 R stay verified; the one loss is the BHAGYANGR p12 R row,
  graded R only because the transcript itself prints the company name as the speaker -- the rule leaves a company
  name as printed and unverified. The NOCIL p1 cover-letter J row is gone; the 11
  P rows stay verified -- five now carry the right speaker (LUMAXIND "Deepak Jain", TDPOWERSYS "Nikhil Kumar", OIL
  "Abhijit Majumder", JUBLINGREA "Ambrish Dixit", TRIVENI "Tarun Sawhney") but their cut/tag faults are outside
  this guard, and REGAAL's "I have to calculate that" answer is not a yes/no reply. Tests: seven in

- **Transcript question_context filled from the printed analyst question (`v2/obs_speaker.py` `preceding_question`, T-242, 25 Nov 2026, DeepSeek).** `hy4_out/obs_transcripts_394_v3.db` holds 26,990 verified statements, 8,597 of them (31.9%) with an empty `question_context`; the strict statement grade counts a referent as present only when `question_context` holds it, so a passage whose only referent is "that"/"it" (or a short yes/no reply) stays P even with the analyst's question printed immediately above it. Script fix, no prompt change: `attach_statement_speakers` fills an EMPTY `question_context` from the printed Q&A. The statement is located on its page with `find_observation_pos`, the printed `Name:` labels before it are walked back at most two pages nearest first, the passage's own turn is skipped (its answer label, or the analyst's label when the answer is unlabelled), and the turn before it must be a questioner -- on the doc's analyst roster, or a speaker the moderator has just announced ("next question is from the line of ...") -- whose text carries a question cue. It is stored as `Q (<name>): <text>`, whitespace-flattened and trimmed to 300 characters. A management turn in that position, a generic role label ("Management:") or opening remarks gives nothing; a model-written question is never replaced. `_flex_regex` normalized straight and curly quotes with two chained `.replace` calls that nested a character class (`['['’]]`), so a statement holding "FY '26" was never found on its page by `find_observation_pos` (which also feeds the speaker carry and the label checks); one `re.sub` per quote form fixes it. Measured on a copy of the v3 DB (`sqlite` backup into `.tmp`, `REALITY_RAW_FETCH=0`, stores `C:/RealityEngine/tg_build/full394`): **385 of the 8,597 empty verified statements filled (4.5%)**; the rest are opening-remarks/presentation passages (no question is printed before them), answers preceded by another manager's turn, and Q&A in calls whose text never assigns a questioner (no roster line and no moderator announcement), so they stay empty by design. The 30-row sample (seed 11, `.tmp/qc_sample30.jsonl`) attaches the question printed immediately before each answer; all 30 read as that answer's question, two of them (TEXRAIL p8) drawn from a two-part question the answer covers in part. A scoped `--reparse` of the same copy (one CAPACITE transcript via `--only`, same stores, no model call) writes 9 questions on that call's page 5 into `.tmp/obs394_v3_cap.db`; the full 9,461-page re-parse runs ~50 minutes and was not finished in this worker's window, so the 4.5% share is the same function applied to every empty verified row of the copy. Tests: five in `reality_engine/tests/test_obs_speaker.py` (same page, previous page, opening remarks, a management label between, the model's question kept). Locally 27 of 30 pass; the other 3 use pytest's `tmp_path` and cannot create it under this worker's sandbox (ACL denial, independent of this change). The v3 transcript fingerprint `2fae7ddac53e` is unchanged (only the prompt template and reader id are hashed).
- **Statement window: sentence snap and referent widen (`v2/obs_speaker.py` `widen_statement`, T-242, 26 Nov 2026, DeepSeek).** The 394 surprise audit 2 (dsh 76ad80de, 53 right / 7 partial / 0 wrong / 0 junk) failed only on statements that began after the words giving them meaning (`this materializes`, `both of these`, `this increase`, `that range`, `The brand`, `those issues`, and a page-opening fragment). Script-only, page-local, no prompt change (v3 window fingerprint `2fae7ddac53e`): `attach_statement_speakers` calls `widen_statement` for every statement before attribution. (1) **Sentence snap:** when the text before the statement in the same printed turn does not end at `. ! ?`, the start moves back to that sentence's start; a page cover/registration/roster block (phone/email/CIN/dial-in lines, a bare `Label:` line, 2+ honourific name lines, a month-date line) is never crossed, a designation label `extract_turns` rejected (`Mr. Karan Bhagat - MD & CEO, 360 ONE WAM:`, rejected there because its hint carries "360") or a section heading (`Outlook: ...`) still bounds the turn, and a printed bullet is dropped from the start. (2) **Referent widen:** when the first sentence opens on / carries an anaphor (pronoun/demonstrative, `both of these`, `the same`, or `the <lowercase common noun>` outside the self-contained list) and `question_context` holds none of its content words, the previous sentence of the same turn is prepended (one sentence, at most 60 words); never across a label, into an analyst's question (the `Q (Name):` in a stored question counts as the asker), or the page start. (3) When neither is possible (the sentence or its referent starts on an earlier page/turn), the statement is left alone and `status_reason` gains a detail note `referent_outside_window:` with `status` unchanged. Measured in-process over every statement (`tasks/t242_statement_window_measure.md`): `tx394_v3_r4.db` 2,537 snapped + 5,026 widened = 7,222 of 55,219 changed (13.08%), 4,697 noted; `fy27_v3_r3.db` 1,159 + 2,836 = 3,821 of 27,951 (13.67%), 2,228 noted; 0 non-verbatim and 0 label crossings in both. Six of the 7 audit rows widen (#6/#20/#26/#27/#29/#30); #37 (POONAWALLA p21) opens mid-sentence at the top of a continued page, so it is noted, not widened, as step 3 requires (widening would pull text from the previous page and break the verbatim-substring invariant). Graded packs: 2 of 48, 5 of 48, 2 of 49 and 5 of 45 R rows change in `regrade_fy27_r3`, `tx394_ckpt6`, `audit_tx394` and `tx_fy27_surprise1` (every change still verbatim and by the same speaker); `regrade_tx394_r4`, `tx394_ckpt7` and `tx394_ckpt8` have no per-row grades file on this laptop and change 7/7/5 rows. Tests: 11 new in `test_obs_speaker.py`.
- **Exact repeat rows are dropped by `--recheck` (`v2/observations.py` `dedupe_repeats`, T-242, 26 Nov 2026).** The live `obs_transcripts_394_v3.db` was reported to hold 29,966 repeats of the same `(doc_key, page, measure, value_text)`. That key also matches distinct rows: statement/guidance rows print no `value_text` (one MACPOWER speaker's 30 different statements on page 24 share it) and two figures can share a value with different labels (`Total 0`, `North 0`). The rule keys the row's whole printed content (doc_key, page, record_type, measure, label/axis, value, unit, scale, period, basis, dimensions, statement, speaker, ...), so only an identical row is a repeat; it keeps the `verified` row first and then the lowest `observation_id`, deletes the rest, runs at the end of `recheck_db` (scoped to the re-checked pages with `--only`) and reports `stats['repeats_dropped']`. Measured: 0 exact repeats in both copy baselines (117,926 and 59,493 rows) and in the live DB (228,601 rows); the narrow key would have deleted 14,558 / 7,159 / 28,860 rows, among them 12,456 / 6,268 / 24,996 distinct statements. Test `test_dedupe_repeats_keeps_the_verified_row_then_the_lowest_id_and_scopes_by_page`. (`docs/FIX_LOOP.md` records the recheck behaviour.)

- **Annual-report row gates (T-242, 24 Nov 2026, `v2/observations.py`).** The fresh strict grade after the statement
  gate was 49 right / 4 partial / 7 wrong (11.7% wrong, bar <= 5%); four wrong kinds were script-catchable from the
  row and its page. `apply_ar_row_gates(rows, page_text, company)` runs for `annual_report` inside
  `apply_number_gates(rows, doc_type, page_text=..., company=...)` (the read path and `--reparse` pass the page text
  and the store company name; `--recheck` calls the gates directly with the page text and the store company), after
  `apply_ar_statement_gate`, and changes none of the existing checks:
  (1) unit -- a unit with no alphabetic unit word (a lone `` ` ``) is gated `ar_unit_unknown`; a blank unit takes the
  page's ONE printed scale note (`(Rs. in lacs)`, `(₹ in million)`, `(Amount in Lakh)`, `(times)`) when the note's
  family fits the measure (a money note never lands on `count.shares`; a `(times)` note needs a ratio measure or a
  ratio/turnover label); a count/capacity noun used as the unit of a money measure ("- vehicle loans" -> unit
  `vehicle`) is gated the same way;
  (2) qualifier label -- an ageing/overdue bucket ("More than 3 years", "Less than 1 year", "0-30 days", "not due")
  or a transaction-phrase-plus-party label ("Purchase of raw materials Beekaylon Synthetics Private Limited") under
  a dictionary headline measure is a slice, not the measure -> `ar_qualified_label`; a party-only label is the row's
  dimension and stays, a NEW/ageing/related-party measure is exempt, and the transaction word must come before the
  party name ("Investment Services Pvt. Ltd." is a party, not a purchase);
  (3) narrative growth -- a percent row whose whole label is change words ("an increase of", "up by", "a growth of")
  names no measure -> `ar_narrative_growth`;
  (4) basis -- the page heading's `standalone|consolidated financial statements` overrides a disagreeing basis
  (`basis_source=page_heading`) when the page names exactly one of them (a page naming both changes nothing); a
  continuation page that prints no basis word anywhere and starts no statement / note / section of its own (none of
  'Notes forming part of', 'Notes to', 'Note N', 'Statement of Profit', 'Balance Sheet', 'Cash Flow', 'Independent
  Auditor' in its first 300 characters) takes the one basis the page before it heads (`previous_text`, passed by the
  read path, `--reparse` and `--recheck`; `basis_source='previous_page_heading'`), and a stated basis is never
  replaced by a carried one.
  Measured on the 60 graded rows of `pilot_ar_reparsed2.db` (`D:/RealityEngine/t242/ar_grade_fresh`, in-process,
  read-only): 5 of the 7 W handled (4 gated -- the ageing bucket, the related-party purchase, the narrative growth,
  the vehicle unit; 1 corrected -- the standalone-note basis) and all 4 P corrected (blank unit -> Lakhs / Times /
  Millions); 0 of the 42 R rows lose verified. The 2 W left are other kinds (bracket sign, year-column swap). On the
  whole 200-page sample (5,607 rows, 1,414 verified): 24 more rows gated (3 narrative, 7 unit, 14 qualifier) and 294
  unit/scale/basis corrections. The unit vocabulary also misses real words ("Percentage", "kilograms"); only a unit
  with no unit word or a label noun is gated (see the `ponytail:` comment in the function), so widening it needs the
  vocabulary, not this gate. Tests `reality_engine/tests/test_ar_sections.py` (6 new tests with their counters); the
  `read_gate` annual_report fingerprint stays `48d75aa69de7` (no prompt or template touched).
  Carried basis (25 Nov, dsh): 181 of 51,306 number rows, on 9 of the 2,256 pages of the 582 annual reports behind
  `obs_ar_fy27.db`, take the page before's heading, and 0 of them print a basis word later on their own page

  5 new tests (`test_ar_row_gate_carries_...`, `..._does_not_carry_onto_a_page_that_starts_a_note_or_statement`,
  `..._does_not_carry_the_basis_when_this_page_names_one_later_on`,
